\displaystyle d_{h}(t;{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}):=\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\!\!\!\!\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\mathbf{w}_{h}:\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Gamma_{h}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{h}})\,d\sigma_{h}+\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\!\!\!\!\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}:\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Gamma_{h}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{h}})\,d\sigma_{h}

\displaystyle c_{h}(t;\mathbf{z}_{h};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}):=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}(({\mathbf{z}}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}})\mathbf{w}_{h})\mathbf{v}_{h}\,d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}(({\mathbf{z}}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}})\mathbf{v}_{h})\mathbf{w}_{h}\,d\sigma_{h}\Big),

\displaystyle c_{h}^{cov}(t;\mathbf{z}_{h};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}):=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{z}}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov})\mathbf{w}_{h})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{z}}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov})\mathbf{v}_{h})\cdot\mathbf{w}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}(\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})\mathbf{z}_{h}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})\mathbf{z}_{h}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}\Big),

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(t;\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}):=\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}q_{h}\,d\sigma_{h}+\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\xi_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}\,d\sigma_{h},

\displaystyle\tilde{b}_{h}(t;{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}):=m_{h}(\xi_{h},\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{n}}_{h})+g_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\xi_{h},\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})+\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{\mathrm{div}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}q_{h}\,d\sigma,

where
\mathcal{B}_{(\cdot)}^{(\cdot)}(\bullet,\bullet)
the discrete deformation tensors are defined in
Lemma

B.1
. Furthermore, we define
{\mathbf{d}}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
as the discrete version of (
B.17
); similar to
d_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
.

Remark 4.2
.

Notice that we have explicitly skew-symmetrized the trilinear forms
c_{h}
and
c_{h}^{cov}
, according to their continuous counterpart in
Remark

3.3
, when
{\bm{\eta}}=0
, which is standard in the literature when using
H^{1}
-conforming Taylor-Hood elements; see
Section

6.2
for more details. More specifically for
c_{h}^{cov}
notice that we have replaced
\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}{\mathbf{P}_{h}}\mathbf{w}_{h}
by the equivalent expression
\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}-(\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})\bm{H}_{h}
; see (
2.8
).

We omit the time-dependency on the bilinear forms, from now unless stated otherwise. Throughout the text we also use the following
energy norm
:

(4.38)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}=||E_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},\qquad||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

Now, with the help of the Transport theorem presented in
Appendix

B
the following Lemma holds.

Lemma 4.6

(Transport Formula Triangulated Surface)
.

Let
\Gamma_{h}(t)
be an evolving admissible triangulation with material velocity
{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N}
, as prescribed in
Section

4.2
. Then, for
f_{h}
sufficiently smooth we have

(4.39)

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}f_{h}\,d\sigma=\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\partial^{\circ}_{h}f_{h}+f_{h}\,\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma_{h}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\,d\sigma.

For
\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}\in\big(S_{h,k_{g}}^{k,C}\big)^{3}
, and
q_{h},\xi_{h}\in S_{h,k_{g}}^{k,C}
we have

(4.40)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}m_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle=m_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})+m_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h})+g_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}),

(4.41)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})

\displaystyle=b_{h}^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\partial^{\circ}_{h}\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})+\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}),

(4.42)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\frac{d}{dt}(\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h},\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h},\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle+(\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h},\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+d_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}),

(4.43)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}a_{S,h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle=a_{S,h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})+a_{S,h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h})+{\mathbf{d}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}),

(4.44)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}a_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle=a_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})+a_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h})+{\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})+{\mathbf{d}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}).

Remark 4.3

(About
{\mathbf{d}}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
)
.

The discrete formula of
{\mathbf{d}}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
can be derived similar to the discrete formula of
d_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
in
Definition

4.1
by considering (
B.17
). For our analysis, we mainly need its bound and eventually some geometric perturbations, see e.g.
Lemma

4.17
. For brevity reasons we make the following compromise:

In general, we see that the structure of
{\mathbf{d}}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
is similar to the
d_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
; see (
B.17
). For that reason, from now on, when we want to carry out calculations for
{\mathbf{d}}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
, we instead do those calculations, first for the easier case
d_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
, and say these results then easily extend to
{\mathbf{d}}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
.

Lemma 4.7

(Transport Formula Lifted Triangulated Surface)
.

Let
\Gamma(t)
be an evolving surface consisting of the lifted triangulation, whose elements move with velocity
{\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N}
as described in
Section

4.4
. Then for For
{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}},\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{w}},\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{v}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))
and
q,\xi,\partial^{\circ}q,\partial^{\circ}\xi\in H^{1}(\Gamma(t))
,

(4.45)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\mathbf{m}}({\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})

\displaystyle={\mathbf{m}}(\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})+{\mathbf{m}}({\mathbf{w}},\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{v}})+{\mathbf{g}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}}),

(4.46)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}b^{L}({\mathbf{w}},\{q,\xi\})

\displaystyle=b^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{w}},\{q,\xi\})+b^{L}({\mathbf{w}},\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\{q,\xi\})+\tilde{b}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{w}},\{q,\xi\}),

(4.47)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\frac{d}{dt}(\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{w}},\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{v}})_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{w}},\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{v}})_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+(\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{w}},\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{v}})_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+d({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}}),

(4.48)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}a_{S}({\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})

\displaystyle=a_{S}(\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})+a_{S}({\mathbf{w}},\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{v}})+{\mathbf{d}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}}),

(4.49)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}a({\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})

\displaystyle=a(\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})+a({\mathbf{w}},\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{v}})+{\mathbf{g}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})+{\mathbf{d}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}}).

We note, once again, that an explicit expression for
{\mathbf{d}}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
is given in (
B.17
). Also, in comparison to
Definition

3.1
we have that

(4.50)

\displaystyle\tilde{b}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{w}},\{q,\xi\}):=m(\xi,{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}})+g({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\xi,{\mathbf{w}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}})+\int_{\Gamma(t)}{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{div}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\nabla_{\Gamma}q\,d\sigma.

4.6.
Properties of the discrete bilinear forms

We now present some known uniform interpolation and Korn-type inequalities that can be found in
[
32
,
50
]
along with some key results involving
discrete weakly tangential divergence-free functions
, i.e.
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}(t)
(
4.5
). We also extend some of those results using Broken Sobolev spaces.

Lemma 4.8

(Interpolation and Korn-type inequalities)
.

There exists
c>0
depending on
\mathcal{G}_{T}
but independent of
h,\,t
, such that he following important uniform inequalities hold,

(4.51)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq c(||E_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+h^{-1}||\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}),

(4.52)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{-2}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ \textnormal{ for all }\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t),

(4.53)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{4}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{1/2}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{1/2}\quad\quad\textnormal{ for all }\mathbf{w}_{h}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t)).

Proof.

Estimates (
4.51
), (
4.52
) follow from
[
32
, Lemma 4.7]
(in our case we make use
Lemma

4.14
where the constant
c
is independent of both
h,\,t
), while (
4.53
) holds due to
[
69
, Lemma 4.2]
.
∎

The following special case regarding the discrete Korn-type inequality, with the help of the broken Sobolev spaces has been proven in
[
33
]
, following similar arguments to
[
44
]
.

Lemma 4.9
.

For
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
, there exists
c>0
depending on
\mathcal{G}_{T}
but independent of
h,\,t
, such that

(4.54)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}_{h}}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\mathcal{T}_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq c\,a_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{w}_{h}),

(4.55)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}_{h}}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{4}(\mathcal{T}_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}.

The following integration by parts formula holds; see
[
32
]
.

Lemma 4.10
.

On the discrete surface
\Gamma_{h}(t)
the following integration by parts formula holds:

(4.56)

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}q_{h}\ d\sigma_{h}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}q_{h}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma_{h}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\ d\sigma_{h}+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{h}}\int_{T}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})q_{h}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma_{h}}{\mathbf{n}}_{h}\ d\sigma_{h}

\displaystyle+\sum_{E\in\mathcal{E}_{h}}\int_{E}[{\mathbf{m}}_{h}]\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}q_{h}\,d\ell,

for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
and
q_{h}\in H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
.

Utilizing all the aforementioned relations and inequalities, we establish the following coercivity and continuity estimates for our discrete bilinear and trilinear forms.

Lemma 4.11

(Bounds and coercivity)
.

The following bounds and coercivity estimates hold:

(4.57)

\displaystyle a_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

(4.58)

\displaystyle a_{S,h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{w}_{h})

\displaystyle\geq||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}^{2}-||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2},

(4.59)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{g}}_{h}(t;{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(4.60)

\displaystyle d_{h}(t;{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

(4.61)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{d}}_{h}(t;{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

(4.62)

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(4.63)

\displaystyle\tilde{b}_{h}(t;{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(4.64)

\displaystyle c_{h}^{cov}(\mathbf{z}_{h};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq||\mathbf{z}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{1/2}||\mathbf{z}_{h}||^{1/2}_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||^{1/2}_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\quad+||\mathbf{z}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{1/2}||\mathbf{z}_{h}||^{1/2}_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{z}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{1/2}||\mathbf{z}_{h}||^{1/2}_{a_{h}},

(4.65)

\displaystyle c_{h}(\mathbf{z}_{h};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq||\mathbf{z}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{1/2}||\mathbf{z}_{h}||^{1/2}_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

for all
\mathbf{z}_{h}(\cdot,t),\mathbf{w}_{h}(\cdot,t),\mathbf{v}_{h}(\cdot,t)\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t).
and
\{q_{h}(\cdot,t),\xi_{h}(\cdot,t)\}\in Q_{h}(t)\times\Lambda_{h}(t),
where the constant
c
is independent of time
t
and the mesh-parameter
h
.

Proof.

The continuity estimates (
4.57
), (
4.62
), and (
4.64
), (
4.65
) have already been proven in
[
33
, Lemma 5.3]
. Specifically for latter two, we just make use of the previous
Lemma

4.9
and the bounds on the discrete Weingarten map
||\bm{H}_{h}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq c
. From
Definition

4.1
, the coercivity estimate (
4.58
) readily follows, while a simple Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the bounds on
{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N}
(
4.8
) along with the smoothness of
{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N}
(see
Section

2.4
) give (
4.59
).

Regarding (
4.60
), we notice that
{\mathbf{P}_{h}}
is symmetric and therefore so is
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Gamma_{h}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{h}})
. So, following the proof of
Lemma

B.3
gives the desired result. The bound (
4.61
), can then be easily extrapolated; see
Remark

4.3
. Finally, (
4.63
) is treated similarly to (
4.62
) (see again
[
33
, Lemma 5.3]
), that is, we use the integration by parts formula (
4.56
), along with the fact that
|[\bm{\mu}_{h}]|\leq ch^{k_{g}}
(
4.16
) and that
{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N}
is the interpolant of the fluid velocity
{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N}
and so the bounds for
{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N}
are as in (
4.8
).
∎

Now we present some key properties involving the
discrete weakly tangential divergence-free functions

\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}(t)
(
6.2
), that will be used throughout. These properties, where proved in
[
33
, Lemma 5.4, Corollary 5.4.1, Lemma 5.5]
, respectively, for stationary surfaces, but can be easily expanded in the evolving setting, so we omit the proofs.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}(t)
and
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
, then the following inequalities hold

(4.66)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch||\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},\ \text{ for }k_{\lambda}=k_{u}.

(4.67)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch||\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},\ \text{ for }k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1,

where
c>0
a constant depending on
\mathcal{G}_{T}
but independent of
h,\,t
.

Corollary 4.12.1
.

Let
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
, then there exists
c>0
depending on
\mathcal{G}_{T}
but independent of
h,\,t
, such that

(4.68)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},\quad\text{ for }k_{\lambda}=k_{u},

(4.69)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},\quad\text{ for }k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1.

Lemma 4.13

(Improved
H^{1}
coercivity bound)
.

If
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
and
{\mathbf{w}}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}(t)
then the following holds

(4.70)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

where
c>0
a constant depending on
\mathcal{G}_{T}
but independent of
h,\,t
.

4.7.
Geometric Perturbations Error Bounds

Let us, first, recall some geometric perturbation results found in several literature
[
28
,
27
,
45
,
44
,
54
,
32
,
50
,
43
]
.

Lemma 4.14
.

For
h
small enough and
\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
,
{\bm{f}}\in(L^{2}(\Gamma(t)))^{3}
, we have the following perturbation bounds, with constant
c>0
depending on
\mathcal{G}_{T}
but independent of
h,\,t

(4.71)

\displaystyle|{\mathbf{m}}({\bm{f}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}({\bm{f}}^{-\ell},\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||{\bm{f}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(4.72)

\displaystyle||(\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell})^{-\ell}-\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(4.73)

\displaystyle|(E({\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{\ell}),E(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}-(E_{h}({\mathbf{w}}_{h}),E_{h}(\mathbf{v}_{h}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(4.74)

\displaystyle||E({\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{\ell})^{-\ell}-E_{h}({\mathbf{w}}_{h})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

Proof.

Refer to
[
54
,
27
]
for the initial two estimates, which can be easily generalized to vector-valued functions, and
[
32
, Lemma 4.6]
for the remaining two (notice that the constant
c
is now independent of both
h,\,t
).
∎

Lemma 4.15

(Lemma 5.8
[
33
]
)
.

The following bound holds for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
,

(4.75)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\beta^{-\ell}\,\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}\,d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma(t)}\beta\,\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\bm{H}{\mathbf{u}}\,d\sigma\Big|\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\beta||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

We now want to compare the bilinear forms on
\Gamma(t)
and
\Gamma_{h}(t)
via the lifting process. We will see that the geometric approximation order of the discrete surface
\Gamma_{h}(t)
, i.e.
{\mathcal{O}}(k_{g})
, appears as a discrepancy error. Before that, for brevity reasons, let us introduce some further notation. For
\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
and
q_{h},\xi_{h}\in H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
we define the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}):={\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})-{\mathbf{m}}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}),\qquad\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{g}}}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}):={\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})-{\mathbf{g}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{a}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}):=a_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})-a({\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{\ell},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}),\qquad\,\quad\mathrm{G}_{d}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}):=d_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})-d({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}):={\mathbf{d}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})-{\mathbf{d}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}),\quad\mathrm{G}_{b^{L}}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}):=b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{w}}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})-b^{L}({\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{\ell},\{q_{h}^{\ell},\xi_{h}^{\ell}\}),

(4.76)

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}):=\tilde{b}_{h}(t;{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})-\tilde{b}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell},\{q_{h}^{\ell},\xi_{h}^{\ell}\}).

Most of the following geometric perturbation error estimates have already been proven in
[
32
,
43
,
27
,
44
]
and references therein, for stationary surfaces, but can be expanded to the evolving setting
[
54
]
; see also
[
54
,
28
,
31
]
regarding the
\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{g}}}
perturbation estimate.

Lemma 4.16

(Geometric Perturbations I)
.

Let
{\mathbf{w}}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
and
\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))\times H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
. Then we have

(4.77)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{a}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(4.78)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{g}}}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{b^{L}}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}(||q_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+||\xi_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))})

(4.79)

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}-1}||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}(||q_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+h||\xi_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}).

If
{\mathbf{w}}\in\bm{H}_{T}^{1}(t)
, then we get the following higher order bound

(4.80)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{b^{L}}({\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})|\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

Furthermore if
{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}}\in\bm{H}_{T}^{1}(t)\cap\bm{H}^{2}(\Gamma(t))
then we have

(4.81)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{b^{L}}({\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(4.82)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{a}({\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell},{\mathbf{v}}^{-\ell})

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{v}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

where, in all the above, the constant
c>0
is independent both of
h
and
t
.

Lemma 4.17

(Geometric Perturbations II)
.

Let
{\mathbf{w}}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
and
\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))\times H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
. Then we have

(4.83)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{d}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(4.84)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}(||q_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+||\xi_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))})

(4.85)

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}-1}||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}(||q_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+h||\xi_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}).

Proof.

Let us start with (
4.83
), then as mentioned in
Remark

4.3
, (
4.84
) follows similarly.
Lifting to
\Gamma(t)
, using (
4.27
) and adding and subtracting
{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}
appropriately we see that

(4.86)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}:\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}=\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma(t)}\frac{1}{\mu_{h}}{\mathbf{P}_{h}}\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}{\mathbf{B}}_{h}^{t}{\mathbf{B}}_{h}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma(t)}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}+\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell},

and using the transport formula (
4.42
) in the left-hand side and lifting once again as above, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}:\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}=\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}:\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}+\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}:\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h}+d_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}+d_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}),

(4.87)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma(t)}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}.

Using now the corresponding lifted transport formula in
Lemma

4.7
we derive that

(4.88)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}Q_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}Q_{h}^{\ell}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}+d({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}),

where
d({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell})=\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\,\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}\,d\sigma+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\,\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}\,d\sigma
; see
Definition

3.1
and
Lemma

B.2
. Furthermore, recalling also (
B.5
) and(
B.6
) we see that

(4.89)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma(t)}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}=\int_{\Gamma(t)}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma(t)}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma(t)}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}.

Combining (
4.7
)-(
4.89
) and (
4.86
), recalling the geometric estimates in
Lemmas

4.1
and
4.2
, the bound for the discrete velocity (
4.8
) and
Lemma

4.11
we deduce

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{d}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})|=\Big|d({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}(\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}))+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma(t)}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{h}^{\ell}\Big|\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

which yields our desired estimate.

Now moving onto (
4.17
) we can readily see from the geometric error (
4.15
) and geometric perturbations (
4.71
), and (
4.78
) that the first two difference terms of
\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}
, which we define as
\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}^{I}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})
; see
Definition

4.1
or (
4.50
), are bounded as desired, that is
\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}^{I}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\xi_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}
.
We are left to bound the difference of the last terms, that is

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{\mathrm{div}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}q_{h}\,d\sigma-\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{div}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}\,d\sigma.

We will follow similar arguments as before. Recall
\mathcal{W}_{h}=\frac{{\mathbf{B}}_{h}}{\mu_{h}}-{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}
and that
\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}q_{h}=\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\frac{1}{\mu_{h}}{\mathbf{B}}_{h}\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}
. Now lifting to
\Gamma(t)
, adding and subtracting
{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}
appropriately and using the transport formulae (
4.41
) (discrete) and (
4.46
) (lifted) (look also (
B.4
)) we derive the following

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}q_{h}=\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\mathcal{W}_{h}\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}+\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma(t)}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\mathcal{W}_{h}\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\mathcal{W}_{h}\nabla_{\Gamma}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}q_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}(\mathcal{W}_{h})\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\mathcal{W}_{h}\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}^{\text{div}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}q_{h}^{\ell}+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}^{\text{div}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}

(4.90)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}q_{h}+\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\partial^{\circ}_{h}q_{h}+\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{\text{div}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}q_{h}.

Now, since
(\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h})^{\ell}=\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}
and
\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}q_{h}=\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\frac{1}{\mu_{h}}{\mathbf{B}}_{h}\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}
by lifting, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{\mathrm{div}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}q_{h}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{div}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\mathcal{W}_{h}\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma}^{\text{div}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N})\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}\Big|+\Big|\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}(\mathcal{W}_{h})\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}\Big|

(4.91)

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||q_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

where we used the fact that
||\partial_{h}^{(k)}\mathcal{W}_{h}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma(t))}\leq ch^{k_{g}}
from (
4.2
). Combining with the bound
\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}^{I}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\xi_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}
, concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.4

(Better Approximations)
.

A question may be whether it is possible to ensure higher order of approximation for the
\mathrm{G}
-terms in
Lemma

4.17
, considering higher regularity tangent vectors as in
Lemma

4.16
. This seems to be a bit complicated.

First, regarding an estimate for
\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}({\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})
it is not difficult to see that the answer is positive. Indeed, since with simple calculations we can see that in (
4.7
) we can replace
\partial_{h}^{(k)}(\mathcal{W}_{h})
by
\partial_{h}^{(k)}({\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathcal{W}_{h})
and therefore the second estimate in (
4.2
)
would yield (after applying inverse ineq.) the better estimate

(4.92)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}({\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

Now, for a result similar to (
4.81
), i.e.

(4.93)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}({\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

as seen in
[
32
, Lemma 6.1]
we would need some sort of
non-standard geometric approximation estimates
similar to
[
43
, Lemma 4.2]
, where now it should be w.r.t. the material derivatives, that is

(4.94)

\displaystyle|(\partial^{\circ}_{h}({\mathbf{P}_{h}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}),\chi^{-\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}|\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||\chi||_{W^{1,1}(\Gamma(t))},\quad|({\mathbf{P}}\partial^{\circ}_{h}({\mathbf{n}}-{\mathbf{n}}_{h}),\chi^{-\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}|\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||\chi||_{W^{1,1}(\Gamma(t))}.

Such estimates have been derived in
[
62
]
, but since they are technical and since we will not use such high order approximations as in (
4.93
), we do not present the proof.

Similarly, to find an estimate as in (
4.82
) for
\mathrm{G}_{d}({\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})
(and therefore for
\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{d}}}({\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})
) with
{\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}}\in\bm{H}_{T}^{1}\cap\bm{H}^{2}(\Gamma)
, that is

(4.95)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{G}_{d}({\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell},{\mathbf{v}}^{-\ell})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{v}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))},

one needs the non-standard estimates (
4.94
) again, but also very heavy computations that mimic the proof in
[
43
, Lemma 5.4, eq. (5.33)]
. Since, again, we will not use this estimate, we do not provide a proof, but do comment that it should be possible to show it. Lastly, we want to note that we can also improve (
4.82
) in the sense that
{\mathbf{w}}

needs not to be tangent
, e.g. for
\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))\cap\bm{H}^{2}(\Gamma(t))
:

(4.96)

|\mathrm{G}_{a}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell},{\mathbf{v}}^{-\ell})\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{v}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

This can be shown similarly to
[
43
, Lemma 5.4]
, where the improvement comes from the that we use the discrete rate-of-strain tensor
E_{h}({\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell})
instead of
E_{h}({\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell})-({\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})\bm{H}_{h}
.

5.
Ritz-Stokes Projection for Evolving Surface Finite Elements

Ritz maps have been studied in the case of elliptic operators on stationary and evolving surfaces
[
28
,
54
,
60
]
, while Ritz-Stokes maps have been studied for stationary domains in the case of no geometric variational crimes, that is,
\Omega_{h}=\Omega

[
6
,
18
,
14
]
and evolving domains
[
70
]
using ALE FEM. In this section, we examine an extended version of the Ritz-Stokes map
\mathcal{R}_{h}(\cdot)

[
33
, Section 5.4]
to evolving triangulated surfaces. The main difference, as we shall see, is that now the normal material derivative and the Ritz-Stokes projection do not commute, i.e.
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}\neq\mathcal{R}_{h}\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}
. However, before we proceed, let us first present the Scott-Zhang interpolant, cf.
[
32
,
43
]
.

5.1.
Scott-Zhang Interpolant

To prove convergence and stability we need to make use of the Scott-Zhang interpolant, and specifically its projection and super-approximation properties. The proofs of
Lemma

4.12
,
Corollary

4.12.1
,
Lemma

4.13
, despite not stating them, rely on this projection since they follow exactly as in
[
33
]
.

Let
\Gamma_{h}(t)
be the interpolated
k_{g}
-order approximation surface of
\Gamma(t)
cf.
Section

4.1
, and let
T\in\mathcal{T}_{h}(t)
a triangulated element. Then, for any
\bm{z}:\mathcal{G}_{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
(scalar :
n=1
or vector-valued :
n=3
) with
\bm{z}\in L^{2}(\Gamma)^{n}
, we define the Scott-Zhang interpolant to be the projection
\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}:(L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t)))^{n}\rightarrow(S_{h,k_{g}}^{k}(t))^{n}
; see
[
43
, Section 4.4]
. Then, the following estimate holds

(5.1)

\displaystyle||\bm{z}^{-\ell}-\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}\bm{z}^{-\ell}||_{H^{l}(T(t))}\leq ch^{k+1-l}||\bm{z}||_{H^{k+1}(\omega_{\tilde{T}}(t))},\quad 0\leq l\leq k+1,\ k\geq 0,

where the constant
c>0
is independent of the mesh parameter
h
and time
t
, but depends on
\mathcal{G}_{T}
, and
\omega_{\tilde{T}}(t)
is a union of neighbouring elements of the element
T(t)
. The following stability bound also holds

(5.2)

||\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}\bm{z}^{-\ell}||_{H^{1}(T)}\leq c||\bm{z}^{-\ell}||_{H^{1}(\omega_{\tilde{T}})}\leq c||\bm{z}||_{H^{1}(\omega_{\tilde{T}}^{\ell})}.

We omit further detail since the extension to evolving surfaces is trivial, but for further information about the construction of the Scott-Zhang interpolant on high-order surfaces, we refer to
[
43
,
32
]
.

Finally, we present super-approximation type of estimates and stability results for the Scott-Zhang interpolant. These type of results were proven in
[
43
]
and made use of the projection property of
\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}(\cdot)
. We extend them to the evolving surface setting.

Lemma 5.1
.

For finite element function
\bm{z}_{h}\in(S_{h,k_{g}}^{k}(t))^{3}
,
\chi\in[W^{3,\infty}(\Gamma(t))]^{3}
and
t\in[0,T]
it holds

(5.3)

\displaystyle\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{h}(t)}||\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(I-\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ})(\chi^{-\ell}\cdot\bm{z}_{h})||_{L^{2}(T)}\leq ch||\chi||_{W^{3,\infty}(\Gamma(t))}||\bm{z}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(5.4)

\displaystyle\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{h}(t)}||(I-\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ})(\chi^{-\ell}\cdot\bm{z}_{h})||_{L^{2}(T)}\leq ch||\chi||_{W^{3,\infty}(\Gamma(t))}||\bm{z}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

where constant
c
is independent of
h,t
, but depends on
\mathcal{G}_{T}
.
The following
L^{2}
stability estimate holds

(5.5)

\displaystyle||\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}(\chi^{-\ell}\cdot\bm{z}_{h})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq c||\chi^{-\ell}\cdot\bm{z}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

5.2.
Surface Ritz-Stokes Projection

Definition 5.1

(Modified Ritz-Stokes projection)
.

Given
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times(L^{2}(\Gamma(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma(t)))
we define by
\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})(t)\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}(t)
,
\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})(t),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})(t)\}\in Q_{h}(t)\times\Lambda_{h}(t)
the unique projection such that

(5.6)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}a_{h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})=a({\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}),\\
b_{h}^{L}(\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})=0,\end{cases}

for all
(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t)\times(Q_{h}(t)\times\Lambda_{h}(t))
. The lift is then defined typically
\big(\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}})(t)
,
\{\mathcal{P}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}})(t),

\,\mathcal{L}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}})(t)\}\big)\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{\ell}(t)\times(Q_{h}^{\ell}(t)\times\Lambda_{h}^{\ell}(t))
.

Definition 5.2

(Ritz-Stokes projection)
.

Given
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times(L^{2}(\Gamma(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma(t)))
we define by
\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}})(t)=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})(t)\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}(t)
,
\mathcal{P}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})(t)=\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})(t)\in Q_{h}(t),\,\mathcal{L}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})(t)=\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})(t)\in\Lambda_{h}(t)
the unique projection such that

(5.7)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}a_{h}(\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\mathcal{P}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})=a({\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})+b^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell},\{p,\lambda\}),\\
b_{h}^{L}(\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})=0,\end{cases}

for all
(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t)\times(Q_{h}(t)\times\Lambda_{h}(t))
. The lift is then defined in the standard way.

As before, we omit the time-dependency of the bilinear forms and the Ritz-Stokes map, unless it is not clear from the context. We also omit, for convenience, the inverse lift extension
(\cdot)^{-\ell}
notation (
4.14
), unless specified, since it should be clear from the context if, e.g., a function
{\mathbf{u}}
is defined on
\Gamma(t)
or
\Gamma_{h}(t)
.

The Ritz-Stokes maps above
\mathcal{R}_{h}^{(\cdot)}({\mathbf{u}})

\big(\!\!:=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})
or
\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}})\big)
are well-defined (see well-posedness and stability in
[
32
, Theorem 5.4]
for stationary case) and the following
a priori
stability estimates hold;

(5.8)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}^{2}+||\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}^{2},

\displaystyle||\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}^{2}+||\{\mathcal{P}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq c\big(||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}+||\{p,\lambda\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}\big).

The following lemma was again shown in
[
33
, Lemma 5.10]
. The extension to evolving surfaces is trivial with the use of
Lemma

4.16
.

Lemma 5.2

(pseudo Galerkin orthogonality)
.

For every
{\mathbf{u}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma)
it holds that

(5.9)

\displaystyle a_{h}(({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h})\leq Ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\quad\text{ for all }\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div},

(5.10)

\displaystyle a_{h}(({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{p,\lambda\}-\{\mathcal{P}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})\leq C^{b}h^{k_{g}-1}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}\quad\text{ for all }\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h},

where the constant
C=c||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}
and
C^{b}=c(||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||p||_{H^{1}}+||\lambda||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)})
independent of
h,\,t
.

Lemma 5.3

(Error Bounds modified Ritz-Stokes)
.

Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times(H^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma(t)))
the solution of (
eNSWd
) or (
eNSWc
) and
t\in[0,T]
. Then, there exists a constant
c>0
independent of
h
and
t
, such that the following error bounds for the Ritz-Stokes projection hold

(5.11)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}+||\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))},

(5.12)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}.

with
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
and
k_{g}\geq 2
and
h\leq h_{0}
with sufficiently small
h_{0}
.

If instead we assume that
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
(that is
V_{h}=\Lambda_{h}
) we obtain the following improved estimates,

(5.13)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}+||\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))},

(5.14)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq c(h^{\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{k_{g}-1})||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))},

(5.15)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+h||({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))},

(5.16)

\displaystyle||({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1/2}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}.

with
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
, for
h\leq h_{0}
with sufficiently small
h_{0}
.

Proof.

Under the assumption that our solution solves (
eNSWd
) or (
eNSWc
) the calculations for the proof are nearly identical to
[
33
, Lemma 5.11]
, where one uses material from the evolving setting instead when applicable.
∎

Lemma 5.4

(Error Bounds Ritz-Stokes)
.

Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times(H^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma(t)))
the solution of (
eNSWd
) or (
eNSWc
) and
t\in[0,T]
. Then, we have the following error bounds for the Ritz-Stokes projection,

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}+||\{p-\mathcal{P}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}),\lambda-\mathcal{L}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq ch^{m}\big(||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}

(5.17)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ \ \ \,+||p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma(t))}+||\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma(t))}\big),

(5.18)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b,\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\leq ch^{m+1}\big(||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}+||p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma(t))}+||\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma(t))}\big),

with
m=min\{r_{u},k_{pr}+1,k_{\lambda}+1\}
,
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
and
k_{g}\geq 2
, for
h\leq h_{0}
with sufficiently small
h_{0}
and with
c
independent of
h
and
t
.

Lemma 5.5

(
L^{\infty}
bound Ritz-Stokes projection;
[
33
]
Lemma 5.13)
.

Given
{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathbf{W}^{2,\infty}(\Gamma)
and
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
or
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
with
k_{g}\geq 2
, there exists a positive constant
c
independent of
h
and
t
such that the following estimate holds

(5.19)

||\mathcal{R}_{h}^{(\cdot)}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+||\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathcal{R}_{h}^{(\cdot)}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}||_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma(t))}.

5.3.
Material Derivative of the Ritz-Stokes map

Since our surface
\Gamma(t)
also depends on time, we require further work to find interpolation error estimates for the material derivative, compared to
[
33
]
. As mentioned before, this arises from the fact that the normal time derivative and the Ritz-Stokes projection do not commute, i.e.
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}\neq\mathcal{R}_{h}\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}
. For convenience, we focus on the
modified Ritz-Stokes
projection; then the general case follows with similar arguments.

We start by taking the time-derivative of (
5.6
) and use the transport formulae in
Lemmas

4.6
and
4.7
to derive the following:

(5.20)

\displaystyle a_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})=\mathrm{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})=\mathrm{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}(\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})+\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{2}(\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}),

for
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
,
\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}
, where recalling the notation in (
4.76
) we have that

\displaystyle\!\!\mathrm{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=a_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h})-a(\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})+{\mathbf{g}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-{\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h})+{\mathbf{d}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-{\mathbf{d}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h})

(5.21)

\displaystyle\!\!\qquad\quad\ \,=\mathrm{G}_{a}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h})+\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{g}}}({\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h})+\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{d}}}({\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}),

(5.22)

\displaystyle\!\!\mathrm{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}(\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}):=-\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}),

(5.23)

\displaystyle\!\!\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):={\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h})+{\mathbf{d}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h})-\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}),

(5.24)

\displaystyle\!\!\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{2}(\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}):=\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}).

Lemma 5.6
.

For sufficiently smooth
{\mathbf{u}}
,
\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}
, the following bounds hold:

(5.25)

\displaystyle\!\!\mathrm{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq c(h^{k_{g}}+h^{k_{u}+1})(||\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}+||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))})||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

(5.26)

\displaystyle\!\!\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},\quad\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{2}(\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\leq ch^{r_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

If furthermore we assume that
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, we obtain

(5.27)

\displaystyle\!\!\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},\quad\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{2}(\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
,
\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}
, where
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
and
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
.

Proof.

Starting with
\mathrm{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})
, using the perturbation estimates for
\mathrm{G}_{a}
(
4.77
),
\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{g}}}
(
4.78
), and
\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{d}}}
, (
4.84
) and the bound (
4.36
) we obtain

(5.28)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq c(h^{k_{g}}+h^{k_{u}+1})(||\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}+||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))})||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}.

Now, focusing on (
5.26
), using the bounds for the bilinear forms (
4.59
), (
4.61
), (
4.63
) coupled with the Ritz-Stokes estimates (
5.11
) yields our desired result.

For the
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
case, i.e. (
5.3
), the estimate for
\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{2}(\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})
is evident if one considers the bound for
\tilde{b}_{h}
(
4.63
) coupled with the Ritz-Stokes estimate (
5.13
) instead.
The
\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})
estimate is a bit more involved, since we now want to utilize
L^{2}\times H^{-1}_{h}
bounds for the pressure (
5.13
). As before, using (
4.59
), (
4.61
) coupled with the Ritz-Stokes estimate (
5.13
) we obtain

(5.29)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+|\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})|.

We need to bound the last term appropriately. Applying (
4.63
) yields

|\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})|\leq c||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+|m_{h}(\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{n}}_{h})|+|g_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})|,

where we see, recalling the definition of the
H_{h}^{-1}
dual norm (
4.6
), that

\displaystyle|m_{h}(\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{n}}_{h})|=m_{h}(\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{n}}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{n}}))+|m_{h}(\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{n}}))|

\displaystyle+|m_{h}(\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{n}})-\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{n}})|

\displaystyle\leq ch||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+c||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{H^{-1}_{h}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{H^{-1}_{h}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},\quad\qquad\qquad\text{ since $||\xi_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq ch^{-1}||\xi_{h}||_{H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}$},

where we also used the geometric errors (
4.15
), the Scott-Zhang super-approximation (
5.4
) and the fact that
\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}(\cdot)\in V_{h}
coupled with the definition of the
H_{h}^{-1}
dual norm (
4.6
). Similarly one bounds
|g_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})|\leq c||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{H^{-1}_{h}(\Gamma_{h})}
. Therefore, by (
5.13
) the above results yield

\displaystyle|\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})|\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

and so, going back to (
5.29
), finally gives our desired estimate.
∎

Now, before moving onto the interpolation error of the material derivative of the Ritz-Stokes projection, let us first consider the following auxiliary Stokes problem (similar to
[
33
, Lemma 5.10]
):

(5.30)

\displaystyle a(\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})\ +b^{L}({\mathbf{v}},\{\partial^{\circ}p,\partial^{\circ}\lambda\})

\displaystyle={\mathbf{m}}(\bm{\mathcal{F}},{\mathbf{v}})\ \ \ \ \ \quad\ \text{for all }{\mathbf{v}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma),

\displaystyle b^{L}(\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}},\{q,\xi\})

\displaystyle=m(\bm{\ell},\{q,\xi\}))\ \ \ \text{ for all }\{q,\xi\}\in(L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma)\times L^{2}(\Gamma)),

where now

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{m}}(\bm{\mathcal{F}},{\mathbf{v}})={\mathbf{m}}(\partial^{\circ}({\bm{f}}-\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{(\cdot)}{\mathbf{u}}-\nabla_{\Gamma}\partial^{\circ}p),{\mathbf{v}})-{\mathbf{d}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N};{\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})-{\mathbf{g}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N};{\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})-\tilde{b}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N};{\mathbf{v}},\{p,\lambda\}),

\displaystyle m(\bm{\ell},\{q,\xi\})=\tilde{b}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N};{\mathbf{u}},\{q,\xi\}).

Then, if we let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
and
(\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}},\{\partial^{\circ}p,\partial^{\circ}\lambda\})
be the solutions of
(
eNSWd
) (or (
eNSWc
)) and its time derivative, respectively, by the transport formulae
Lemma

3.1
, it is clear that
(\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}},\{0,0\})
is the solution of (
5.30
). We may then consider the standard finite element approximation of (
5.30
),
(\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h},\{\partial^{\circ}_{h}p_{h},\partial^{\circ}_{h}\lambda_{h}\})
which satisfies the following

(5.31)

\displaystyle a_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}_{h},{\mathbf{v}}_{h})\ +b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{v}}_{h},\{\partial^{\circ}_{h}p_{h},\partial^{\circ}_{h}\lambda_{h}\})

\displaystyle={\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }{\mathbf{v}}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h},

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})

\displaystyle=m_{h}(\bm{\ell}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\ \ \ \text{ for all }\{q,\xi\}\in(Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}),

where now
\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{h}:=\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{-\ell}
and
m_{h}(\bm{\ell}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}):=-\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})
. We select
\bm{\ell}_{h}
as such, so that
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
; see (
5.20
). Refer also to
Remark

5.3
for other choices. Finally, set

{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{\tilde{b}_{h}}(t):=\{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t):\,b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{v}}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})=-\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}),~~\forall\,\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}(t)\times\Lambda_{h}(t)\}.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))
satisfy (
5.30
), with
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
as in
Lemma

5.3
, and
(\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h},\{\partial^{\circ}_{h}p_{h},

\partial^{\circ}_{h}\lambda_{h}\})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t)\times(Q_{h}(t)\times\Lambda_{h}(t))
satisfy (
5.31
). Then, for
t\in[0,T]
and
h\leq h_{0}
with sufficiently small
h_{0}
, we have the following bounds,

(5.32)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+h||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{a_{h}}\leq ch^{r_{u}+1}\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}.

If furthermore, we assume that
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
(that is
V_{h}=\Lambda_{h}
) we obtain the following extra estimates

(5.33)

\displaystyle||(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h})\cdot{\mathbf{n}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq ch^{\widetilde{r}_{u}}\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))},

\displaystyle||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq ch^{r_{u}}\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))},

where
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
and
\widetilde{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u}+1/2,k_{g}\}
.

Proof.

We focus on the
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
case, since
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
follows in a similar way, where one uses the appropriate estimates relevant to this case.
The following calculations are similar to
[
32
, Lemma 6.12, Theorem 6.13, Corollary 6.1]
.

Considering (
5.30
) and (
5.31
), we apply the results in
[
32
, Theorem 6.11,Corollary 6.1]
, and with our choice of
\bm{\ell}_{h}
and standard perturbation estimates, we readily see that

(5.34)

||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq ch^{k_{g}}(||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+||\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))})+\!\!\!\!\inf_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{\tilde{b}_{h}}}||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}.

Therefore, it remains to bound the last term in (
5.34
). Using the
L^{2}\times L^{2}

inf-sup
(
6.40
) and recalling
[
32
, Lemma 6.12]
or
[
35
, Lemma 5.5]
, we see that for
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
there exists a unique
\mathbf{z}_{h}\in({\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div})^{\perp}
such that

(5.35)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{z}_{h}||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}}\frac{b_{h}^{L,*}(\mathbf{z}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})}{||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}},

(5.36)

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L,*}(\mathbf{z}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})

\displaystyle=b_{h}^{L,*}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})-b_{h}^{L,*}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})-\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}),

where
b_{h}^{L,*}
the adjoint of
b_{h}^{L}
; see
[
32
, Lemma 6.12]
. Furthermore, using the fact that
\frac{d}{dt}b^{L}({\mathbf{u}},\{q,\xi\})=0
for all
\{q,\xi\}\in L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma(t))
, the transport formula (
4.46
) and setting a new pressure variable
q_{h}^{\prime}=q_{h}^{\ell}+m_{q}\in L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma(t))
for
m_{q}=-\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\int_{\Gamma(t)}q_{h}^{\ell}\,d\sigma
(notice that
\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\prime}=\nabla_{\Gamma}q_{h}^{\ell}
) we get

\displaystyle\big|-b_{h}^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})-\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\big|\leq\big|-b_{h}^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})+b^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h}^{\prime},\xi_{h}\})\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\big|-b^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h}^{\prime},\xi_{h}\})-\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|-b_{h}^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})+b^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\big|+\big|\tilde{b}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h}^{\ell},\xi_{h}^{\ell}\})-\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\big|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}-1}||\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}(||q_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+h||\xi_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))})+|\mathrm{\mathbf{R}_{2}}(\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})|+|\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}({\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})|

(5.37)

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}}(||\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))})(||q_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+h||\xi_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}),

where for the second inequality we used the second line of the perturbation estimate (
4.16
), while in the third inequality we utilized the bound of
\mathrm{\mathbf{R}_{2}}
in (
5.3
) and the perturbation estimate (
4.17
).

Then using the fact that
b_{h}^{L,*}
is the adjoint of
b_{h}^{L}
, by integration by parts (
4.56
), going back to (
5.35
), we may derive the following, after taking the infimum over all
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
,

(5.38)

||\mathbf{z}_{h}||_{a_{h}}\leq\inf_{\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+ch^{k_{g}-1}(||\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}).

Similarly to the above calculations, if we use the
L^{2}\times H_{h}^{-1}
inf-sup
(
6.41
) instead we obtain

(5.39)

||\mathbf{z}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq\inf_{\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+ch^{k_{g}-1}(||\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}).

Setting now
\mathbf{v}_{h}=\mathbf{z}_{h}+\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{\tilde{b}_{h}}
by a simple triangles inequality we then obtain

\displaystyle||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\leq||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+||\mathbf{z}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

\displaystyle||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}({\Gamma_{h}(t)})}

\displaystyle\leq||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}({\Gamma_{h}(t)})}+||\mathbf{z}_{h}||_{H^{1}({\Gamma_{h}(t)})},

from which, taking the infimum over all
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
and
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{\tilde{b}_{h}}
, considering (
5.38
), (
5.39
) and inserting it into (
5.34
), the bound in (
5.32
), (
5.33
) for the
a_{h}
-,
H^{1}
-norms follow (e.g.
\mathbf{w}_{h}=\widetilde{I}_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}})
the standard Lagrange interpolant). Having these results, the perturbation estimates
Lemmas

4.16
and
4.17
, and Ritz-Stokes estimate
||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1/2}
(
5.15
), (
5.16
), the rest of the estimates, involving the tangential and normal
L^{2}
norm, are obtained almost identically as in
[
32
, Theorem 6.14, 6.16]
.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

Notice that for
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, the convergence is worse than that proved in
[
32
]
. This is due to the fact that
\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}\neq 0
; see also
Remark

5.4
for further details. For the approximation
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}
in (
5.30
) we could have chosen
\bm{\ell}_{h}:=\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})
instead. Then, the calculations would change, since now
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\notin{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
(see also (
5.44
) below where we use this fact), but ultimately the convergence would still not improve.

Now we are ready to prove the error bounds for the time derivative of the Ritz-Stokes projection.

Lemma 5.8

(Error Bounds in Material Derivative modified Ritz-Stokes map)
.

Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times(H^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma(t)))
the solution of (
eNSWd
) or (
eNSWc
) and
t\in[0,T]
. Then, for
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
or
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
and
k_{g}\geq 2
, the following error bounds for the normal discrete time derivative of the Ritz-Stokes projection hold,

(5.40)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!||\partial^{\circ}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{a_{h}}+||\{\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}.

Furthermore if specifically
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, the subsequent bounds also hold

(5.41)

\displaystyle||\partial^{\circ}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))},

(5.42)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+||\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))},

with
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
,
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
and
c>0
is independent of
h
,
t
.

Proof.

Once again, we focus on the
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
case. The other case can be derived analogously using the same ideas and applying the appropriate estimate concerning the
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}.

For the energy estimate
, adding and subtracting the Galerkin approximation
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{\tilde{b}_{h}}(t)
in (
5.31
) we see, by the bound (
5.32
), that

(5.43)

\displaystyle\!\!\!||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle=a_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h})+a_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{r_{u}}||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}+a_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})),

where the constant
C=\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}
. We are left to bound the last term. For this, recall equation (
5.20
) and observe that for a test function
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
the following holds true

(5.44)

\displaystyle a_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\mathrm{\mathbf{R}}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

where we used the proven bounds (
5.25
) and (
5.3
), coupled with the
H^{1}
coercivity estimate (
4.70
)
(||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq c||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}})
. Furthermore, by construction of
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}
in (
5.31
) we have that
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}(t)
. From these two fact we readily see that

(5.45)

\displaystyle a_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))\leq Ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}\big(||\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}||_{a_{h}}+||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}\big).

So going back to (
5.43
), substituting (
5.45
) and using Young’s inequality and a simple kickback argument yields our desired result for the energy estimate in (
5.40
).

For the
H^{1}
-estimate
, using the bound (
5.33
) for
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}
and the
H^{1}
coercivity estimate (
4.70
), since by construction
\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
, we obtain

(5.46)

\displaystyle||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}||_{a_{h}},

from which (
5.41
) follows, since
||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}||_{a_{h}}\leq Ch^{r_{u}}
as proved before.

For the pressure estimates
we just go back to (
5.20
), solve for
b_{h}^{L}
and use the
L^{2}\times H_{h}^{-1}
discrete Lagrange
inf-sup
condition in
Lemma

6.7
. So, using (
5.25
), (
5.3
) and the energy estimate in (
5.40
), which we proved beforehand, implies that

\displaystyle||\{\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{a_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h})+\mathrm{{\mathbf{G}}_{1}}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\mathrm{\mathbf{R}_{1}}(\mathbf{v}_{h})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+Ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}.

Using the
L^{2}\times L^{2}

inf-sup
in
Lemma

6.6
, instead, and the worse
H^{1}
coercivity estimate (
4.52
), also proves the estimate (
5.40
):

(5.47)

||\{\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq ch^{r_{u}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}.

Now we are left to find
the tangential and normal
L^{2}
-norm error bounds
of the material derivative of the velocity, i.e. (
5.42
). For the tangential part, as in
[
33
, Lemma 5.11]
, we use a duality argument. So, let us consider

({\mathbf{w}},\pi,\mu)\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma(t))
that satisfy

(5.48)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}a({\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})\ +\!\!\!\!&b^{L}({\mathbf{v}},\{\pi,\mu\})=({\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}(\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}{\mathbf{u}}),{\mathbf{v}})\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }{\mathbf{v}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t)),\\
&b^{L}({\mathbf{w}},\{\sigma,\xi\})=0\ \ \ \text{ for all }\{\sigma,\xi\}\in L_{0}^{2}(\Gamma(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma(t)),\end{cases}

for
t\in[0,T]
. Now, the surface
\Gamma
is sufficiently smooth and thus due to
[
68
, Lemma 2.1]
the solution also satisfies the following higher regularity estimate

(5.49)

||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}+||\{\pi,\mu\}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}\leq||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}(\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}{\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}.

Set
{\mathbf{e}}_{h}=\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}
. Then, testing with
{\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell}=\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}{\mathbf{u}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))
, we clearly see that

(5.50)

\displaystyle\boxed{||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}{\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}=a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{\mathbf{w}})+b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{\pi,\mu\}).}

We need to bound the two terms on the right-hand side of (
5.50
) appropriately. This follows
[
33
, Lemma 5.11]
but now, since the non-tangentiality of
\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}
limits us to geometric approximation error
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{g}-1})
in the energy (
a_{h}
) velocity and
L^{2}\times H_{h}^{-1}
pressure norm (
5.40
), we should expect to obtain an improved bound for the tangential
L^{2}
-norm with geometric approximation error
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{g}})
(instead of the
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{g}+1})
in the stationary case
[
33
, Lemma 5.11]
); see
Remark

5.4
for more details. Moreover, due to the transport formulae used to derive (
5.20
), there are also terms that prevent us from improving the degree of velocity approximation, yielding sub-optimal
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{u}})
order of convergence. Such term is
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,9}
in (
5.59
). For more details see
Remark

5.5
.
So, moving on to the calculations, for the two bilinear terms on the right-hand side of (
5.50
) we write

(5.51)

\displaystyle a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{\mathbf{w}}-{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))}_{\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{1}^{a}}+\underbrace{a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))-a_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))}_{\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{2}^{a}}+\underbrace{a_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))}_{\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a}}

\displaystyle b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{\pi,\mu\})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{\pi-{I}_{h}(\pi),\mu-{I}_{h}(\mu)\})}_{\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{1}^{b}}+\underbrace{b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{{I}_{h}(\pi),{I}_{h}(\mu)\})-b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})}_{\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{2}^{b}}

(5.52)

\displaystyle\ +\underbrace{b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})}_{\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{b}},

where
\widetilde{I}_{h}(\cdot)
the standard Lagrange interpolant. Let us now bound each term separately. Recall that for
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
we have shown
||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{a_{h}}\leq||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq ch^{r_{u}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}
:

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\bullet\quad\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{1}^{a}=a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{\mathbf{w}}-{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))

\displaystyle\leq c||{\mathbf{w}}-{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

(5.53)

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))},

where we used the fact that
||\cdot||_{a}\leq c||\cdot||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}\leq c||\cdot||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}
, see (
4.31
) (one can also use the fact that
||\cdot||_{a}\leq||\cdot||_{a_{h}}
, see (
4.73
), but holds only for
k_{g}\geq 2
