), and standard nodal interpolation estimates.

\displaystyle\bullet\quad\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{2}^{a}

\displaystyle=a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))-a_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}

(5.54)

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+r_{u}}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}\qquad(\text{where we used }\eqref{eq: Geometric perturbations a ENS}).

Now recalling the definition (
5.20
), where now
\mathbf{v}_{h}=\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})\neq{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
, we reformulate it as followed

\displaystyle\ \bullet\quad\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{G}_{a}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))+\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{g}}}({\mathbf{u}},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))+{\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})-{\mathbf{w}})+{\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle+\mathrm{G}_{{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))+{\mathbf{d}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))+{\mathbf{d}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})-{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle+{\mathbf{d}}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),{\mathbf{w}})-\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}),\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})-b_{h}^{L}(\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}),\{\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})

(5.55)

\displaystyle:=\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,i}\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{for }i=1,...,10.

From geometric perturbations
Lemmas

4.16
and
4.17
(Notice
\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}\neq 0
), the bounds on the bilinear forms
Lemma

4.11
, the Ritz-Stokes interpolation estimates in (
5.13
), (
5.15
) (notice that
||{\mathbf{P}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}
and
{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}{\mathbf{w}}={\mathbf{w}}
) and the estimate (
4.35
), we see that

(5.56)

\displaystyle|\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,i}|\leq c(h^{k_{g}}+ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1})\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))},\text{ for }i=1,...,7,

where we also use the standard Lagrange interpolation estimates. We are left to bound the last line of (
5.55
), i.e.
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,i}
,
i=8,9,10
. These require a bit more calculations.

As done before, cf.
Remark

4.3
, instead of bounding
{\mathbf{d}}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
, let us just consider
d(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
instead for clarity reasons. Then the bound for
{\mathbf{d}}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
will follow. Similar to
[
28
, Theorem 6.2]
we need to perform integration by parts. Recall from
Definition

3.1
:

\displaystyle d({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}),{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Gamma(t)}\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}})):\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{w}}\,d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma(t)}\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}})):\nabla_{\Gamma}{\mathbf{w}}\,d\sigma.

Using Einstein summations, see
Appendix

A
and (
A.1
), we define
\{B\}_{ik}=\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})
and
\{z\}_{k}=\{u-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}(u)\}_{k}
, then, using the integration by parts formula (
2.11
), the first integral can be written as

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma(t)}B_{ik}(\nabla_{\Gamma})_{j}(z_{k})P_{mi}(\nabla_{\Gamma})_{j}(w_{m})=\int_{\Gamma(t)}(\nabla_{\Gamma})_{j}\big(B_{ik}z_{k}P_{mi}(\nabla_{\Gamma})_{j}(w_{m})\big)-\int_{\Gamma(t)}z_{k}(\nabla_{\Gamma})_{j}\big(B_{ik}(\nabla_{\Gamma})_{j}(w_{m})P_{mi}\big)

(5.57)

\displaystyle=-\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma(t)}\kappa n_{j}\big(B_{ik}z_{k}P_{mi}(\nabla_{\Gamma})_{j}(w_{m})\big)}_{=0}-\int_{\Gamma(t)}z_{k}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(\tilde{(\mathcal{B}}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{w}}){\mathbf{e}}_{k})

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma(t)}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))\cdot\mathrm{\mathbf{div}_{\Gamma}}((\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N},{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}})\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{w}})^{t})\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1/2}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))},

where
{\mathbf{e}}_{k}
denotes the standard unit vector, and where we used the fact that
||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1/2}
by (
5.16
), (
5.15
). One can bound the second integral similarly. Therefore, we can readily see that
d({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}),{\mathbf{w}})\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1/2}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}
, and with a simple extrapolation argument we derive

(5.58)

\displaystyle|\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,8}|\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1/2}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}.

Noticing that
b^{L}({\mathbf{w}},\{\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{P}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{L}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})=0
and the already proven estimate (
5.47
), we readily see,

\displaystyle|\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,10}|

\displaystyle\leq\big|b_{h}^{L}(\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})-{\mathbf{w}},\{\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})\big|+\big|{\mathbf{G}}_{b}({\mathbf{w}},\{\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{P}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\mathcal{L}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})\big|

\displaystyle\leq c(h^{r_{u}+1}+ch^{k_{g}+r_{u}})\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}.

Finally, using the bound (
5.13
) and similar calculations to (
5.29
)-(
5.3
) for
\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}),\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})
we are only able to derive the following

(5.59)

\displaystyle|\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,9}|\leq c||\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})||_{a_{h}}||\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

where notice the low convergence order
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{\widehat{r}_{u}})
, and note how it restricts the improvement in the velocity approximation; see
Remark

5.5
for more details.
Therefore,
combining all
|\mathcal{I}_{3}^{a,i}|

i=1,...,10
above into (
5.55
) we obtain

(5.60)

\displaystyle\boxed{|\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a}|\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.}

Now moving onto the
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{j}^{b}

j=1,...,3
bounds, by the standard interpolation estimates we see that

\displaystyle\ \bullet\quad\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{1}^{b}=b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{\pi-{I}_{h}(\pi),\mu-{I}_{h}(\mu)\})

\displaystyle\leq c||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}||\pi-{I}_{h}(\pi)||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+c||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mu-{I}_{h}(\mu)||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}+1}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}(||\pi||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||\mu||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}).

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07164pt\bullet\quad\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{2}^{b}

\displaystyle=b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{{I}_{h}(\pi),{I}_{h}(\mu)\})-b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}(||\pi||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||\mu||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)})

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+r_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}(||\pi||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||\mu||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)})\qquad(\text{where we used the perturbation }\eqref{eq: Geometric perturbations btilde ENS}),

where we also used the norm equivalence (
4.31
) where appropriate. Finally, recalling the definition of
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})
(see second line of (
5.20
)) and following the calculations in (
5.37
), we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt\bullet\quad\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{b}

\displaystyle=b_{h}^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})-b_{h}^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})

\displaystyle=b_{h}^{L}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})+\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})

\displaystyle\leq|\mathrm{G}_{b^{L}}(\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})|+|\mathrm{G}_{\tilde{b}}({\mathbf{u}},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})|+|\mathrm{\mathbf{R}_{2}}(\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}||\{\pi,\mu\}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+c||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\{\pi,\mu\}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

where we also used the perturbation bounds (
4.17
), (
4.16
) and the bound
\mathrm{\mathbf{R}_{2}}(\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})=\tilde{b}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}||\{\pi,\mu\}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}
. Then, using once again the fact that
||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1/2}
(see (
5.16
), (
5.15
)) we derive:

\boxed{|\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{b}|\leq c(h^{k_{g}}+h^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1/2})\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}||\{\pi,\mu\}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.}

By substituting
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{j}^{a},\,\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{j}^{b}
,
j=1,2,3
into (
5.51
) and (
5.52
)
and subsequently to (
5.50
), we obtain

(5.61)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}{\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\leq c(h^{k_{g}}+h^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1/2}+h^{\widehat{r}_{u}})\sum_{j=0}^{1}||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}(||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}+||\{\pi,\mu\}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}),

from which, recalling the regularity estimate (
5.49
), yields the tangent part our desired estimate (
5.42
).

The normal part of (
5.42
) can easily be derived by using (
5.33
) and (
4.68
). Indeed, we just rewrite
\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}=\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{\ell})\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}+\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{\ell}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}
and use the fact that
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathbf{u}_{h}-\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
.

Now, let us briefly discuss the
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
case. As mentioned in the beginning of this proof, the
(a_{h})
-velocity and
L^{2}
-pressure bounds follow similarly, where one uses the respective estimates, e.g. the worse
H^{1}
coercivity estimate (
4.52
) instead of (
4.70
) and the bounds (
5.26
). Unfortunately, for the tangential
L^{2}
-norm of the velocity, we are unable to find an improved bound for two main reasons: First, as discussed, the term
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,9}
(
5.55
) will now only give a bound of
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{r_{u}})
and second, the term
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,8}
, and specifically (
5.57
), which will also give a bound of order
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{r_{u}})
, due to the suboptimal
L^{2}
-norm estimate
||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\leq ch^{r_{u}}
in (
5.40
). For more details, see
Remark

5.5
.
∎

Remark 5.4

(Geometric error sub-optimality of
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})
)
.

In
Lemma

5.8
we observe suboptimal convergences results w.r.t. the geometric error, which stems from the non-tangentiality of the material derivative
\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}
. Indeed, in the stationary case
[
33
, Lemma 5.10]
, for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
we were able to prove
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{g}})
and
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{g}+1})
geometric error convergence, for the time-derivative, since
\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}=0
when
{\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}=0
. However, in an evolving setting, this is no longer true, as it is obvious that if
{\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}=0
then
\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}\neq 0
. That means, theorems as in
[
32
, Lemma 6.12]
(see proof of
Lemma

5.7
in our case) and perturbation results, cf. (
4.81
), (
4.82
) and
Remark

4.4
, that depend on the tangentiality of the arguments, no longer hold for
\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}
. Therefore, in general, we lose one geometric order of convergence.

Remark 5.5

(Issues regarding tangential
L^{2}
-norm of the Material Derivative of Ritz-Stokes maps)
.

Let us talk more extensively about the issues regarding the sub-optimality of the tangential
L^{2}
-norm convergence of
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})
, and why we are unable to prove better results. As mentioned in
Remark

5.4
the reason for the loss of geometric error is the non-tangentiality of the material derivative, i.e.
\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}\neq 0
. However, we also notice no improvement in the velocity approximation order
k_{u}
.

\bullet

\boxed{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
: Notice that we only improve the geometric approximation order up to
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{g}})
. This is apparent from the terms
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,1}
,
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,5}
, in (
5.55
) and
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{b}
, where we can only show
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{g}})
convergence due to the restrictive bounds (
4.77
), (
4.84
) and (
4.16
) respectively. We believe that
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,1}
,
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,5}
can be improved by
Remark

4.4
, since we can rewrite
\mathrm{G}_{(\cdot)}(\cdot,\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))=\mathrm{G}_{(\cdot)}(\cdot,\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})-{\mathbf{w}})+\mathrm{G}_{(\cdot)}(\cdot,{\mathbf{w}})
, with
{\mathbf{w}}\in\bm{H}^{1}_{T}\cap\bm{H}^{2}
. Finally, we notice that only the term
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,9}
in (
5.55
) is responsible for the lack of improvement in the velocity approximation order
k_{u}
. This term appeared only due to the transport formulae used to derive (
5.20
). We believe that this can be improved, and so all in all obtain an an improved bound
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{u}+1/2})
for the tangential Ritz-Stokes projection.

\bullet

\boxed{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
: In this case, we actually expect
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{g}})
geometric error (see
[
33
, Lemma 5.10, eq. (5.42)]
or
Lemma

5.3
), thus the previous issues in
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,1}
,
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,5}
and
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{b}
do not concern us. Despite that a new
issue
arises in term
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,8}
, since now by
Eq.

5.57
we can only obtain a bound w.r.t. the full
L^{2}
-norm of
{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})
, which in this case by (
5.40
), gives the suboptimal estimate
||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\leq ch^{r_{u}}
. Therefore, this term limits us both in the geometric
k_{g}-1
and velocity approximation order
k_{u}
, unless it is possible to perform calculations such that
||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}
appears instead. The issue regarding the term
\bm{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{a,9}
still remains.

Despite the above, in the numerical experiments (
Section

8.2
) we observe higher convergence (
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1/2})
, (
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{r_{u}+1/2})
) for the tangential
L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}
(or
L^{2}_{L^{2}}
)-norm of the velocity in both cases, which indicates that our estimates for the material derivative of the velocity might not be sharp; as we also suspected.

Now for the the material derivative of the standard Ritz-Stokes projection
\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}})
in
Definition

5.2
, it is not difficult to see that the calculations follow exactly as in the modified Ritz-Stokes case, where now we take the time derivative of (
5.7
) instead. The extra term on the right-hand side does not cause any problems and can be treated easily with already established estimates. So, for the sake of brevity we omit the proof and provide the following Ritz-Stokes error bounds.

Lemma 5.9

(Error Bounds for Material Derivative Ritz-Stokes)
.

Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times(H^{1}(\Gamma(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma(t)))
the solution of (
eNSWd
) or (
eNSWc
) and
t\in[0,T]
. Then, we have the following error bounds for the material derivative of the Ritz-Stokes projection,

\displaystyle||\partial^{\circ}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{a_{h}}+||\{\partial^{\circ}_{h}(p-\mathcal{P}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}})),\partial^{\circ}_{h}(\lambda-\mathcal{L}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}))\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq ch^{m}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\big(||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}

(5.62)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ \ +||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma(t))}+||(\partial^{\circ})^{j}\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma(t))}\big),

with
m=min\{r_{u},k_{pr}+1,k_{\lambda}+1\}
,
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
and
k_{g}\geq 2
, for
h\leq h_{0}
with sufficiently small
h_{0}
and with
c
independent of
h
and
t
.

6.
The Fully Discrete Scheme

As mentioned in
Section

4.2

we consider the
Taylor-Hood
surface finite elements
\mathrm{\mathbf{P}}_{k_{u}}
–
\mathrm{P}_{k_{pr}}
–
\mathrm{P}_{k_{\lambda}}

for a
k_{g}
-order approximation of the surface, and set

k_{u}\geq 2,~k_{pr}=k_{u}-1~~\mbox{and}~~k_{\lambda}\geq 1.

We maintain the notation
k_{g},k_{u},k_{pr}
and
k_{\lambda}
in order to track how error dependencies arise and define the following finite element spaces

(6.1)

V_{h}(t)=S_{h,k_{g}}^{k_{u}}(t),~~{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t)=(S_{h,k_{g}}^{k_{u}}(t))^{3},~~Q_{h}=S_{h,k_{g}}^{k_{pr}}(t)\cap L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma_{h}(t)),~~\Lambda_{h}(t)=S_{h,k_{g}}^{k_{\lambda}}(t).

In this section, we consider two different numerical schemes that are based on the two weak formulations (
eNSWd
), (
eNSWc
) discussed in
Section

3.3
. For the discrete scheme based on (
eNSWd
) we are able to show stability and optimal convergence results only when
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
using
iso-parametric surface finite elements
. On the other hand, for the second scheme, we can establish optimal results for both
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
and
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
using
iso-parametric
and
super-parametric discretizations
, respectively (compare (
4.70
) and (
4.52
) from which the geometric order loss emerges). Moreover, the latter choice of
k_{\lambda}
also requires further regularity assumptions for optimal pressure results; see
2
.

Let us also recall the space of
discrete weakly tangential divergence-free functions

(6.2)

{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}(t):=\{\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t):b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})=0,~~\forall\,\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}(t)\times\Lambda_{h}(t)\}.

and the dual space of the finite element space
\Lambda_{h}(t)
, denoted as

H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
in (
4.6
).

6.1.
Further Notation: Time – Discretization

In what follows, we introduce some further notation needed to formulate and analyze the fully discrete scheme. We split the time interval
I=[0,T]
uniformly into
N>0
time intervals
I_{n}=[t_{n},t_{n-1}]
with nodes
t_{n}=n\Delta t
, where
\Delta t
is the uniform timestep
\Delta t=T/N
. We also use the shorthand
(\cdot)^{n}
for any quantity calculated at timestep
t_{n}
, e.g.
\Gamma_{h}^{n}:=\Gamma_{h}(t_{n})
,
{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}:={\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t_{n})
, etc. For a discrete-time sequence
f^{n},\,n=0,1,...,N

we also use the shorthand notation

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}f^{n}=\frac{1}{\Delta t}\big(f^{n}-f^{n-1}\big).

Following the notation in
Section

4.2
, let

\{\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k,n}\}_{j=1}^{J}=\{\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k}(\cdot,t_{n})\}_{j=1}^{J}
and
\{(\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k,n})^{\ell}\}_{j=1}^{J}=\{\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k,\ell}(\cdot,t_{n})\}_{j=1}^{J}
be the nodal basis functions of
S_{h,k_{g}}^{k,n}=S_{h,k_{g}}^{k}(t_{n})
and
(S_{h,k_{g}}^{k,n})^{\ell}=S_{h,k_{g}}^{k,{\ell}}(t_{n})
respectively. Then, as in
[
29
,
34
]
we employ the following notation for
z_{h}^{n}\in S_{h,k_{g}}^{k,n}
and
z_{h}^{n,\ell}\in(S_{h,k_{g}}^{k,n})^{\ell}
:

(6.3)

\displaystyle z_{h}^{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}z_{j}^{n}\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k,n},\qquad z_{h}^{n,\ell}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}z_{j}^{n}(\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k,n})^{\ell}.

It is also convenient to define functions whose values are in a previous timestep, e.g.
t_{n-1}
, but belong to the next surface, e.g.
\Gamma_{h}^{n}
. Hence, we may define for
\alpha=-1,0
and
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
the
time-lift
:

(6.4)

\displaystyle\underline{z_{h}^{\!n+\alpha}}(\cdot,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{J}z_{j}^{\!n+\alpha}\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k}(\cdot,t)\in S_{h,k_{g}}^{k}(t),\quad\underline{z_{h}^{n+\alpha,\ell}}(\cdot,t)=\sum_{j=1}^{J}z_{j}^{\!n+\alpha}\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k,\ell}(\cdot,t)\in S_{h,k_{g}}^{k,\ell}(t).

So, for example,
\underline{z_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})\in S_{h,k_{g}}^{k}(t_{n-1})
defined on
\Gamma_{h}^{n-1}
and
\underline{z_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})\in S_{h,k_{g}}^{k}(t_{n})
defined on
\Gamma_{h}^{n}
. We now introduce the time-discrete material derivative, where given
z_{h}^{n}\in S_{h,k_{g}}^{k,n}
we have

(6.5)

\partial^{\circ}_{h,\tau}z_{h}^{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\partial_{\tau}z_{j}^{n}\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k,n}\in S_{h,k_{g}}^{k}(t_{n}),\quad\partial^{\circ}_{\ell,\tau}z_{h}^{n,\ell}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\partial_{\tau}z_{j}^{n}(\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k,n})^{\ell}\in S_{h,k_{g}}^{k,\ell}(t_{n}).

As in the continuous case the
transport property
(
4.11
) (
4.34
) (cf.
[
29
]
) still holds for the fully discrete material derivative of the basis functions, therefore for
n=0,1,...,N
we have

(6.6)

\partial^{\circ}_{h,\tau}\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k,n}=0,\quad\partial^{\circ}_{\ell,\tau}(\chi_{j,k_{g}}^{k,n})^{\ell}=0.

From (
6.4
), it is obvious that on
[t_{n-1},t_{n}]

(6.7)

\partial^{\circ}_{h}\underline{z_{h}}^{\!n+\alpha}(\cdot,t)=0,\quad\partial^{\circ}_{\ell}\underline{z_{h}}^{\!n+\alpha,\ell}(\cdot,t)=0,

which, implies that

(6.8)

z_{h}^{n}=\underline{z_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})+\Delta t\,\partial^{\circ}_{h,\tau}z_{h}^{n}.

All the above naturally extend to vector-valued functions, e.g.
\bm{z}_{h}^{n}\in(S_{h,k_{g}}^{k}(t_{n}))^{3}
.

6.2.
The Fully Discrete Scheme

We consider two numerical schemes which are based on the two different weak formulations (
eNS
) and (
eNSc
) examined in
Section

3.3
, where we assume that

\boxed{\bm{\eta}=0.}

Refer to
Remark

3.2
and the weak formulations (
eNSWd
), (
eNSWc
). The reasoning for considering this condition is explained in further detail in
Remark

6.1
.

So now, with the above assumptions, the discrete bilinear forms in
Definition

4.1
(see also
Remarks

3.3
and
4.2
regarding the skew-symmetrization of
c_{h}
and
c_{h}^{cov}
), the transport formula (
4.39
) and (
6.8
) in mind, we proceed to define our fully discrete approximation based on (
eNSWd
):

The linearized
directional evolving fully discrete Lagrangian surface Navier-Stokes
finite element approximation is the following.

(eNSW
d
h
):

Given
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{0,div}
and appropriate approximation for the data
{\bm{f}}_{h}
, for
n=0,1,...,N
find
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}
,
\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}^{n}\times\Lambda_{h}^{n}
such that

(eNSW
d
h
)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\Delta t}\Big({\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}))\Big)-{\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})+a_{S,h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,+c_{h}(\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})={\mathbf{m}}_{h}({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}),\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \!b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\})=0.\end{cases}

for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}
and
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n-1}
and
\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}^{n}\times\Lambda_{h}^{n}
. Here, in our trilinear form
c_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
we used the
time-lift
(
6.4
)
\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}
in the first argument
to linearize our formulation. We may consider this
time-lift
as an interpolation of the previous solution to the next surface.

We utilize the same idea to define the next fully discrete approximation scheme based on the weak formulation (
eNSWc
): The linearized
covariant evolving fully discrete Lagrangian surface Navier-Stokes
finite element approximation is the following:

(eNSWc
h
):

Given
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{0,div}
and appropriate approximation for the data
{\bm{f}}_{h}
, for
n=0,1,...,N
find
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}
,
\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}^{n}\times\Lambda_{h}^{n}
such that

(eNSWc
h
)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\Delta t}\Big({\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}))\Big)-{\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})+a_{S,h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,+c_{h}^{cov}(\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})={\mathbf{m}}_{h}({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}),\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \!b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\})=0.\end{cases}

for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}
and
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n-1}
and
\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}^{n}\times\Lambda_{h}^{n}
. Again, the finite element scheme is linearised with the help of the time-lift
\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})
.

In both schemes, we have chosen
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{0,div}
for the initial data so that our schemes remain unconditionally stable (no inverse CFL condition is required). Some examples include
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}:=\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
or
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}:=\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0},\{p^{0},\lambda^{0}\})
for
{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma)
; see
Remark

7.1
for further details. In practice (
eNSWdh
) may be more natural and convenient to implement compared to (
eNSWch
), since less geometric information is needed for the approximation of the convective term, cf.
Section

8.1
.

Note that in both schemes (
eNSWdh
) and (
eNSWch
) the same notation
\lambda_{h}
is used for convenience, although they approximate different functions (depending on the choice of the convective term
c_{h},
or
c_{h}^{cov}
); see also
Sections

3.1
and
3.3
.
Note that, see
Remark

6.2
,

•

For (
eNSWdh
), we are only able to provide stability and convergence results when
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
.

•

For (
eNSWch
) we are able to perform a complete analysis for both
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
and
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
/

For that reason, from now on,
when studying (
eNSWdh
) we only consider
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, whilst
when analyzing (
eNSWch
) we only consider
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
.

Remark 6.1

(About the non-homogeneous source term (
3.11
))
.

As discussed in
Remark

3.2
, in the analysis we set (
3.11
)
{\bm{\eta}}=(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})=0
. This reduces the problem to one with divergence-free and tangential constraints on the velocity
{\mathbf{u}}
. A similar condition was imposed in
[
69
]
, where, the authors required a homogeneous divergence condition, since it was not clear how to found an appropriate discrete bound for the source term. In our case, we set

\bm{\eta}=0
, first for the sake of concision and brevity, since in proving Ritz-Stokes estimates
Lemma

5.8
the computations are already quite heavy. Second, our equations now satisfy the condition
{\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}=0
, and therefore we can take advantage of the said tangentiality in our analysis; see for example
Lemma

4.16
,
Lemma

5.3
and the results in
[
32
, Lemma 6.1, Lemma 6.12]
. In practice, we implement a discretization of the standard surface Navier–Stokes equations (
eNSW
); see
Section

8.1
and (
LMM
) where optimal convergence are observed.

Remark 6.2
.

For the first discrete scheme, we can carry out our analysis (mainly regarding the pressure) only when
\Lambda_{h}
is sufficiently rich, i.e.,
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
. In this case, the improved
H^{1}
-coercivity estimate (
4.70
) now holds yielding the following equivalence relation between the directional and covariant derivative:

\displaystyle||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\sim||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}\qquad\text{ for }\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}(t).

This implies the crucial observation that the convective term
c_{h}
is bounded w.r.t. the
a_{h}
-norm, cf. (
4.65
). In the
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
case, we have only the worse
H^{1}
-coercivity estimate (
4.52
) and, in our analysis, this results in a loss of an order of convergence.

On the other hand, we can prove stability and optimal convergence results for both
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
and
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
, in the case of the (
eNSWch
) scheme, since the convective term
c_{h}^{cov}
can be bounded appropriately; see the new bound (
4.64
) which emerges due to the term
\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{u}_{h}
. For that reason, in our analysis, cf.
Section

7.3
, when considering
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
we focus on the (
eNSWch
) scheme.

6.3.
Discrete properties of bilinear forms

In this section we recall and extend results found in
[
34
,
29
]
regarding some discrete properties of bilinear forms
Definition

4.1
.
To start with, we extend the results in
[
29
, Lemma 3.6]
for vector-valued functions, for time
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n+1}]
as in
[
34
]
, with the help of the transport formulae
Lemma

4.6
, and the bounds
Lemma

4.11
.

Lemma 6.1
.

For
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n+1}]
and
\mathbf{w}_{h}(t)\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t)
we have the estimates

(6.9)

\displaystyle|{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\underline{\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t),\underline{\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t))|\leq c\Delta t\,{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}),

(6.10)

\displaystyle|a_{S,h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n})-a_{S,h}(\underline{\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t),\underline{\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t))|\leq c\Delta t\,a_{S,h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n})\leq c\Delta t\,a_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}),

(6.11)

\displaystyle|a_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n})-a_{h}(\underline{\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t),\underline{\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t))|\leq c\Delta t\,a_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}),

(6.12)

\displaystyle|b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})-b_{h}^{L}(\underline{\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t),\{\underline{p_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t),\underline{\lambda_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t)\})|\leq\Delta t\,||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

and so for any
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n+1}]
and
\Delta t
sufficiently small, it holds

(6.13)

\displaystyle||\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t)||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

(6.14)

\displaystyle||\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t)||_{a_{S,h}}

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{S,h}},

(6.15)

\displaystyle||\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t)||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}.

Proof.

Eq. (
6.9
)-(
6.11
) and (
6.13
)-(
6.15
) are natural vector-valued extensions of
[
29
, Lemma 3.6]
, where one uses the new transport formulae
Lemma

4.6
and bounds
Lemma

4.11
. For (
6.12
), using calculations similar to the previously proven estimates, i.e. the transport formula (
4.41
), the bound (
4.63
), and the already proven time inequalities (
6.13
), (
6.15
), give the final result.
∎

From (
eNSWdh
), (
eNSWch
) we see that the solution
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
. An essential issue is that, since the computational surface is time-varying, we have to deal with the fact that we need divergence conformity of velocity solutions at two different time-steps (surfaces). But, for
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n-1,div}
, using the
time-lift
(
6.4
) we see that
b_{h}^{L}(\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n});\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\})\neq 0
for
\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}^{n}\times\Lambda_{h}^{n}
, and so it is not
discretely weakly tangential divergence-free
with respect to
\Gamma_{h}^{n}
, i.e.
\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})\notin{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
.
However, since we use
evolving surface finite elements
(ESFEM) we have, by the time-lift (
6.4
) again, that

(6.16)

\boxed{b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1},\{\underline{q_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}),\underline{\xi_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})\})=0\quad\text{ for all }\{\underline{q_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}),\underline{\xi_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})\}\in Q_{h}^{n-1}\times\Lambda_{h}^{n-1}.}

This important
property
will be used throughout the text to help tackle the aforementioned issue.

In the upcoming
Section

6.4
in order to find pressure stability it is important that the previous time-step is in
{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
. For that reason, we define a
time discrete version of the surface Leray projection
.

Definition 6.3

(Leray time-projection)
.

For
\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n-1,div}
, we define
\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div},

\{\hat{p}_{h}^{n},\hat{\lambda}_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}^{n}\times\Lambda_{h}^{n}
such that it is the unique solution of

(6.17)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1},{\mathbf{v}}_{h}^{n})+b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{v}}_{h}^{n},\{\hat{p}_{h}^{n},\hat{\lambda}_{h}^{n}\})

\displaystyle={\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{{\mathbf{v}}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}))

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1},\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\})

\displaystyle=0,

for all
{\mathbf{v}}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n},

\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}^{n}\times\Lambda_{h}^{n}
.

This time-projection is clearly well-posed. Furthermore, by (
6.16
) it has the following properties.

Lemma 6.2
.

For
\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n-1,div}
and
\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
and
\Delta t
sufficiently small we have

(6.18)

\displaystyle||\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},

(6.19)

\displaystyle||\{\hat{p}_{h}^{n},\hat{\lambda}_{h}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq||\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+c\Delta t||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},

(6.20)

\displaystyle||\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq c\Delta t||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}.

Proof.

For the first inequality we just test (
6.17
) with
\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}
, therefore we see that

\beta||\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq||\underline{\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}\underset{\eqref{eq: time equivalence norms ENS}}{\leq}||\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},

which concludes our assertion. The inequality (
6.19
) then holds if we simply use the discrete
inf-sup
(
6.40
), add and subtract suitable terms and apply (
6.9
), to see that

\displaystyle||\{\hat{p}_{h}^{n},\hat{\lambda}_{h}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}}\frac{{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}-\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}}+\frac{{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{{\mathbf{v}}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}))}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}}

\displaystyle\leq||\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+c\Delta t||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}.

Now to show (
6.20
) we notice from (
6.17
) that

(6.21)

{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})={\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{{\mathbf{v}}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}))-b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{v}}_{h}^{n},\{\hat{p}_{h}^{n},\hat{\lambda}_{h}^{n}\}).

Taking
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}=\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}
in (
6.21
), observing from (
6.16
), (
6.12
), (
6.17
) that

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1},\{\hat{p}_{h}^{n},\hat{\lambda}_{h}^{n}\})

\displaystyle=b_{h}^{L}(\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\{\hat{p}_{h}^{n},\hat{\lambda}_{h}^{n}\})-\underbrace{b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1},\{\underline{\hat{p}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}),\underline{\hat{\lambda}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})\})}_{:=0\text{ since }\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n-1,div}\eqref{eq: blb important time property ENS}}

\displaystyle\leq c\Delta t||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}||\{\hat{p}_{h}^{n},\hat{\lambda}_{h}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

and using the prior proven estimate (
6.19
) and (
6.9
) once again, we obtain the following

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1})\leq c\Delta t||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}||\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle+\Delta t||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}||\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+c(\Delta t)^{2}||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}.

Then, from the fact that
||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}\leq||{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}
, an application of Young’s inequality and a kickback argument gives our desired result.
∎

6.4.
Well-posedness

We now prove stability results for our discrete
velocities

\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}
and the two
pressures

\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}

n=0,...,N
. For the pressures, we mainly study the (
eNSWdh
) scheme and so consider
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, as mentioned in
Remark

6.2
.
Nevertheless, in
Remark

6.6
we also show how one can establish pressure stability when
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
for the scheme (
eNSWch
) by imposing additional conditions.

6.4.1.
Well-posedness of the velocity

We start with the velocities, where we make use of the following matrix formulation (
6.22
) of (
eNSWdh
) and (
eNSWch
):
Consider
\{{\bm{\chi}}_{j}^{n}\}_{j=1}^{J}=\{\{\chi_{j}^{n}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}\}_{j=1}^{J}
,
\{\psi_{j}^{n}\}_{j=1}^{J}
and
\{\varphi_{j}^{n}\}_{j=1}^{J}
to be the nodal basis of
{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}
,
Q_{h}^{n}
and
\Lambda_{h}^{n}
respectively. Then, we can write the following
matrix-vector formulation
of the finite element approximation (
eNSWdh
):Determine the vector-matrix
{\mathbf{U}}^{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{J\times 3}
and coefficient vectors
Q^{n},\,\Lambda^{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}
, given
{\mathbf{U}}^{n-1}
such that they solve

(6.22)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta t}({\mathbf{M}}^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}-{\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{n-1})-{\mathbf{G}}^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}+\hat{{\mathbf{A}}}^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}+\mathbf{C}^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}+{\mathbf{B}}^{n}\{Q^{n},\Lambda^{n}\}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{F}}^{n}

\displaystyle B^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}

\displaystyle=0,

where
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}{\bm{\chi}}_{j}^{n}
and
q_{h}^{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}Q^{n}\psi_{j}^{n}
,
\lambda_{h}^{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\Lambda^{n}\varphi_{j}^{n}
and where the time-depending matrices are defined as

(6.23)

\displaystyle({\mathbf{M}}^{n})_{ij}

\displaystyle:={\mathbf{m}}_{h}(t_{n};{\bm{\chi}}_{i}^{n},{\bm{\chi}}_{j}^{n}),\quad\ (\hat{{\mathbf{A}}}^{n})_{ij}:=a_{S,h}(t_{n};{\bm{\chi}}_{i}^{n},{\bm{\chi}}_{j}^{n}),\qquad\quad\ \ ({\mathbf{A}}^{n})_{ij}:=(\hat{{\mathbf{A}}}^{n})_{ij}+({\mathbf{M}}^{n})_{ij}

\displaystyle({\mathbf{G}}^{n})_{ij}

\displaystyle:={\mathbf{g}}_{h}(t_{n};{\bm{\chi}}_{i}^{n},{\bm{\chi}}_{j}^{n}),\qquad\!(\mathbf{C}^{n})_{ij}:=c_{h}(t_{n};\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}};{\bm{\chi}}_{i}^{n},{\bm{\chi}}_{j}^{n}),\qquad({\mathbf{F}}^{n})_{ij}:={\mathbf{m}}_{h}(t_{n};{\bm{f}}_{h,i}^{n},{\bm{\chi}}_{j}^{n})

\displaystyle({\mathbf{B}}^{n})_{ij}

\displaystyle:=b_{h}^{L}(t_{n};{\bm{\chi}}_{i}^{n},\{\varphi_{j}^{n},\psi_{j}^{n}\}).

One can define the
matrix-vector formulation
of (
eNSWch
) analogously.

Lemma 6.3

(Velocity Estimate)
.

For the velocity solution
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{k}
,
k=0,1,...,n
of (
eNSWdh
) or (
eNSWch
) we have the following energy estimates, which holds for
n\geq 1

(6.24)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq exp(ct_{n})\big(||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{0})}^{2}+\Delta t\sum_{k=0}^{n}||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{k}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{k})}^{2}\big),

where
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div,0}
an appropriate approximation
{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma)
, e.g.
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}:=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0})
, and where the constant
c>0
is independent of
h,\,t
.

Proof.

Let us consider the
matrix-vector formulation
(
6.22
) and the matrices (
6.23
), where
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}{\bm{\chi}}_{j}^{n}
and
q_{h}^{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}Q^{n}\psi_{j}^{n}
,
\lambda_{h}^{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\Lambda^{n}\varphi_{j}^{n}
. Testing this equation with
{\mathbf{U}}^{n}
we obtain

(6.25)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta t}({\mathbf{M}}^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}-{\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{n-1})\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}+{\mathbf{A}}^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}={\mathbf{F}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}+{\mathbf{G}}^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}+{\mathbf{M}}^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n},

where we used the fact that
c_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
is skew-symmetric and
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
, therefore
\mathbf{C}^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}=B^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot\{Q^{n},\Lambda^{n}\}=0
, and that
a_{h}=a_{S,h}+{\mathbf{m}}_{h}
. Using the classic identity
({\mathbf{a}}-{\mathbf{b}})\cdot{\mathbf{a}}=\frac{1}{2}({\mathbf{a}}^{2}-{\mathbf{b}}^{2})+\frac{1}{2}({\mathbf{a}}-{\mathbf{b}})^{2}
, the first two term can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{\mathbf{M}}^{n}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}-{\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{n-1}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}-{\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{n-1}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}+({\mathbf{M}}^{n}-{\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}){\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}

(6.26)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big({\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}-{\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{n-1}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n-1}\big)

\displaystyle\ +\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}({\mathbf{U}}^{n}-{\mathbf{U}}^{n-1})\cdot({\mathbf{U}}^{n}-{\mathbf{U}}^{n-1})+({\mathbf{M}}^{n}-{\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}){\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}^{n}.

Now the last term above is bounded using (
6.9
) by

(6.27)

({\mathbf{M}}^{n}-{\mathbf{M}}^{n-1}){\mathbf{U}}^{n}\cdot U^{n}\geq-c\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2},

while by the fully discrete time derivative (
6.8
) and the time-lift (
6.4
) we see that the first term in the last line of (
6.4.1
) can be written as

(6.28)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}m_{h}(\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})=\frac{(\Delta t)^{2}}{2}m_{h}(\underline{\partial^{\circ}_{h,\tau}\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}),\underline{\partial^{\circ}_{h,\tau}\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})).

So applying (
6.4.1
), (
6.27
) and (
6.28
) to the main equation (
6.25
), along with the bound (
4.59
) for
{\mathbf{g}}(\bullet,\bullet)
and the
\epsilon
-weight Young’s inequality, we can rewrite (
6.25
) as

(6.29)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}-||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}^{2}\big)+\frac{(\Delta t)^{2}}{2}m_{h}(\underline{\partial^{\circ}_{h,\tau}\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}),\underline{\partial^{\circ}_{h,\tau}\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}))+\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon)\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+\frac{\Delta t}{\epsilon}||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}.

Finally summing the inequalities for
k=1,2,...,n
, and applying a discrete Grönwall argument, for small enough time step
\Delta t
, we obtain our desired result. A similar stability bound is obtained for (
eNSWch
), since the only change is the convective term
c^{cov}(\bullet;,\bullet,\bullet)
which is still skew-symmetric.
∎

6.4.2.
Well-posedness of the pressure

Before proving pressure stability bounds we highlight two points.

•

First, further assumptions are needed regarding the velocity approximation. As in the proof for the stationary surface case
[
33
]
, we require that
\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})
in
c_{h}^{(\cdot)}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
is uniformly bounded in the energy
(a_{h})
-norm (
4.38
) for each for each
n=1,...,N
, that is noting (
6.15
)

(6.30)

\sup_{n}||\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})||_{a_{h}}\leq\sup_{n}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}\leq\sup_{n}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}\leq C_{a},

for some constant
C_{a}>0
.
Assuming this, allows us to to prove stability results for the two pressures (in reality when
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
; for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
see
Remark

6.6
where further assumptions are needed), which otherwise would not be possible, due to the skew-symmetric form of
c_{h}^{(\cdot)}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
and the
H^{1}-
conforming Taylor-Hood finite elements.

However,
we are able to show that solutions of the initial approximation of the surface Navier-Stokes problem (
eNSWdh
), i.e.
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}
, actually satisfy (
6.30
), under standard regularity conditions on the true velocity solution
{\mathbf{u}}
. This becomes clear upon proving velocity error estimates in
Section

7.1
, with the help of the Ritz-Stokes projection defined in equation (
5.6
); see
Remark

7.2
for further details.

•

Second, there is the complication that
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}\notin{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
, cf.
Section

6.3
, since the computational surface is time-varying. This issue has been raised in the literature
[
76
,
64
,
15
]
in the case of unfitted and Eulerian time-stepping schemes on evolving domains and in
[
69
]
in the case of evolving surfaces, where it was, therefore, not possible to compute standard stability or convergence estimates for the
L^{2}_{L^{2}}
-norm of the pressure. To circumvent it, the authors instead use a weaker discrete
L^{1}_{H^{1}}
semi-norm coupled with an added inverse CFL condition, where they managed to establish stability – error bounds solely in that weaker norm.
However, in our case, since we are using
ESFEM
, we are able to find an
L^{2}_{L^{2}}
estimate for pressure
p_{h}^{n}
with the help of the
Leray time-projection
(
6.17
) and its properties
Lemma

6.2
(see also (
6.16
)).

In the stationary case, both for surfaces
[
33
]
and domains
[
18
,
51
]
, the authors find an auxiliary bound for the time-derivative using the inverse Stokes operator. Now, due to the evolving setting, we utilize both the
Leray time-projection
(
6.17
) and a
discrete inverse Stokes operator

\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}:\,{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}\rightarrow{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
, defined by the following unique solution
to the discrete Stokes problem: Given
\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
, consider

(6.31)

\displaystyle a_{h}(\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\})

\displaystyle=0,

for every
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
and
\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}^{n}\times\Lambda_{h}^{n}
. It is clear that the operator is coercive and also bounded, thus, it is well-defined. We can also see by testing appropriately that

(6.32)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}||_{\mathcal{A}_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle=||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}=(\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},\qquad||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}||_{\mathcal{A}_{h}}=||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}\leq||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}.

This allows us to establish the following
time-projected
discrete time-derivative auxiliary result.

Lemma 6.4

(Auxiliary bound)
.

Let assumption (
6.30
) hold. Then for the solution
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{k}
of (
eNSWdh
) the following estimate holds for
n\geq 1

(6.33)

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Delta t||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}^{k}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{k-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq C(C_{a}){\mathbf{A}}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0},{\bm{f}}_{h}),

where
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div,0}
an appropriate approximation, e.g.
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}:=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0})
,
{\mathbf{A}}={\mathbf{A}}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0},{\bm{f}}_{h})
the bound in (
6.24
) and
C(C_{a})
the constant depending on the assumption (
6.30
), independent of
h
,
t
.

Proof.

Testing the fully discrete scheme (
eNSWdh
) with
\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
, which by (
6.31
) is well-defined, since
\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div},
and recalling the Leray time-projection (
6.17
), yields
for
1\leq n\leq N

\displaystyle{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}))-{\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}))+a_{S,h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}))

(6.34)

\displaystyle+c_{h}(\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n});\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}))=({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

Now, Let us bound each term appropriately:

\bullet\
By the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{h}
(
6.31
) we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}))=\frac{1}{\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})||^{2}_{a_{h}}.

\bullet\
By the bounds (
4.59
), (
4.57
),
||\cdot||_{a_{S,h}}\leq||\cdot||_{a_{h}}
and Young’s inequality we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}))

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq c\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+\frac{1}{5\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2},

\displaystyle a_{S,h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}))

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{S,h}}||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{S,h}}

\displaystyle\leq c\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\frac{1}{5\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

\bullet\
Recalling the assumption (
6.30
), the bound (
4.65
), and using Young’s inequality we
see that

\displaystyle c_{h}(\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}))

\displaystyle\leq||\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{a}\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\frac{1}{5\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

\bullet\
Similar calculations give the following

\displaystyle({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\leq c\Delta t||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+\frac{1}{5\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

The combination of the above results in (
6.4.2
) yields

\frac{1}{\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})||^{2}_{a_{h}}\leq C_{a}\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+c\Delta t||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2},

where summing this inequality for
n=1,2,...,\,k
and recalling the velocity bounds in
Lemma

6.3
concludes our assertion.
∎

Now, we need to bound the fully discrete time-derivative
\frac{1}{\Delta t}\Big({\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}))\Big)
in a negative norm (dual
H^{1}
-norm), with respect to already known bounds, e.g. the auxiliary bound for
||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}
(
6.33
), and (
6.24
).
Similar estimates have been considered in
[
18
,
19
,
6
,
51
]
for the Oseen and Navier–Stokes problems, where the authors prove a similar
H^{-1}
-norm bound on fixed domains without variational crimes involving domain approximation. These estimates were expanded in
[
33
]
for
k_{g}
-order approximated surfaces
\Gamma_{h}
. Now, we further extend this result, with the help of the
Leray time-projections
(
6.17
) for evolving triangulated surfaces
\Gamma_{h}(t)
of order
k_{g}
. The following proof utilizes the result in
[
33
, Lemma 6.5]
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume that
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, then for a vector-valued functions
\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
the following estimate holds for constant
c>0
independent of
t,\,h
:

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}}\frac{{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t^{n-1}))}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}}

\displaystyle\leq ch||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+c||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}

(6.35)

\displaystyle\quad+c\Delta t||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}.

Proof.

We start by rearranging, adding and subtracting appropriate
L^{2}
-terms on different surfaces
\Gamma_{h}^{n},\,\Gamma_{h}^{n-1}
, so that with the help of the Leray time-projection (
6.17
), we can define
weakly tangential divergence-free functions
on the same time-surface, and hence make use of
[
33
, Lemma 6.5]
. So, for
\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
and
\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
, cf. (
6.17
), we derive the following

(6.36)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}))

\displaystyle={\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})+{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}-\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})

\displaystyle+{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})).

The second term on the right-hand side of (
6.36
) can be bounded using (
6.20
) by

(6.37)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}-\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})\leq c\Delta t||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

while using the time-difference bound (
6.9
) the last two terms are bounded by

(6.38)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\underline{{\mathbf{w}}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}))\leq c\Delta t||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}.

Now, as we mentioned, since
\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
, by
[
33
, Lemma 6.5]
we can readily see that the first term can be bounded as

(6.39)

\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}}\frac{{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}}\leq ch||\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+c||\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{w}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}.

Now combining (
6.37
)-(
6.39
) and (
6.36
), and taking the supremum over
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}
gives our result.
∎

Now we move on to prove stability estimates for the two pressures
\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}
.
First, we present the two discrete
inf-sup
conditions, which play an important role. These were proven in
[
32
, Lemma 5.6, Lemma 5.8]
for stationary surfaces, but can be easily be extended to the evolving setting.

Lemma 6.6

(
L^{2}\times L^{2}
Discrete Lagrange
inf-sup
condition)
.

Assume a quasi-uniform triangulation of
\Gamma_{h}
, then there exists a constant
\beta>0
independent of the mesh parameter
h
and the time
t
, but dependent on
\mathcal{G}_{T}
, such that

(6.40)

\beta||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))\times L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t)}\frac{b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}}\ \ \ \forall\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}(t)\times\Lambda_{h}(t).

Lemma 6.7

(
L^{2}\times H_{h}^{-1}
Discrete Lagrange
inf-sup
condition)
.

Assume a quasi-uniform triangulation of
\Gamma_{h}
, then there exists a constant
\beta>0
independent of the mesh parameter
h
and the time
t
, but dependent on
\mathcal{G}_{T}
, such that

(6.41)

\beta||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}(t)}\frac{b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}}\ \ \ \forall\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}(t)\times\Lambda_{h}(t).

Lemma 6.8

(
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{H_{h}^{-1}}
Pressure Bounds)
.

Let
Lemma

6.7
and the assumptions in
Lemma

6.4
and
Lemma

6.5
hold. Then, for the pressure solutions
\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}\in Q_{h}^{k}\times L_{h}^{k}
,
k=1,...,n
of (
eNSWdh
) the following stability estimate holds true

(6.42)

\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{k})\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}^{k})}^{2}\leq C(C_{a}){\mathbf{A}}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0},{\bm{f}}_{h}),

where
{\mathbf{A}}={\mathbf{A}}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0},{\bm{f}}_{h})
the bound in (
6.24
) and
C(C_{a})
a constant depending on the assumption (
6.30
).

Proof.

Recall the discrete
inf-sup
condition in
Lemma

6.7
, then we have

(6.43)

\displaystyle||\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}}\frac{b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}}.

Using the weak formulation (
eNSWdh
) along with the bounds in
Lemma

4.11
and the
H^{1}
-coercivity bound (
4.70
), where appropriate (
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
), we see that

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})\leq-\dfrac{1}{\Delta t}\Big({\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}))\Big)+c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle+c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}+||\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}.

Returning to (
6.43
), using the dual inequality (
6.5
) and recalling the assumption (
6.30
) we obtain

(6.44)

\displaystyle||\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq ch||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+c||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}+c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle+c(1+C_{a})||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}+||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq c||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}+c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}+c(1+C_{a})||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}+||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

where in the last inequality we used the definition of the discrete inverse Stokes operator
\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}
(
6.31
), the fact that
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
and the inverse inequality to show that

\displaystyle||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}\leq ch^{-1}||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

hence
h||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\leq c||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}.
Now squaring and then multiplying (
6.44
) by
\Delta t
, simple calculations yield

\displaystyle\Delta t||\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq c\Delta t||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}^{n}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\hat{{\mathbf{u}}}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+C_{a}\Delta t(||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2})+||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}.

Finally, summing for
k=1,...,n
and using the velocity and auxiliary bounds (
6.24
) and (
6.33
) respectively, we obtain our final result.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

Notice that in
Lemma

6.8
using the
inf-sup
condition (
6.41
), we were only able to show
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{H_{h}^{-1}}
stability bounds for the pressures. This is because in our numerical scheme (
eNSWdh
) the bound of the trilinear form
c_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
is given by (
4.65
) and therefore the test function
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}
is bounded w.r.t. the
H^{1}
-norm. If instead the (
eNSWch
) scheme was used (the one we use for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
), then the bound of
c_{h}^{cov}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
is given by (
4.64
) and therefore, since now the test function is bounded by the energy
(a_{h})
-norm instead, we would have been able to use the
L^{2}\times L^{2}
discrete
inf-sup
condition (
6.40
), which in turn would provide us with
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
pressure bounds. This follows similarly to
[
33
]
.

Remark 6.5
.

In the case that the right-hand side of (
eNSWdh
) was not zero, but some approximation function
{\bm{\eta}}_{h}
of
{\bm{\eta}}
in (
eNSW
), then in the stability results the following extra terms would arise

(6.45)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big(\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}||\partial^{\circ}_{h}{\bm{\eta}}_{h}(\cdot,t)||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}+||{\bm{\eta}}_{h}(\cdot,t)||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\,dt\big).

Remark 6.6

(About
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
pressure stability for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
)
.

Now we briefly wish to discuss the
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
pressure stability for when
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
. First, as previously mentioned in
Remark

6.2
we use the finite element formulation (
eNSWch
). Second, the dual estimate (
6.5
) only holds for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
and therefore we need an alternative approach. Basically, instead of the auxiliary result (
6.33
) we need to find a stability estimate for the stronger expression

(6.46)

\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\partial^{\circ}_{h,\tau}\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\partial^{\circ}_{h,\tau}\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n})=\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}))}{(\Delta t)^{2}}.

This is almost identical to
[
33
, Remark 6.1]
for stationary surfaces where one requires a bound for
\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{n}||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}
.
However, to establish this result, we first need stronger assumptions regarding
\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})
, as opposed to (
6.30
). Mainly that

(6.47)

\sup_{n}||\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq\sup_{n}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq C_{a}^{\infty}.

We will see that this new assumption holds as long as
further regularity assumptions
are imposed; see
Remark

7.3
. So assuming and using (
6.47
), we can then just test (
eNSWch
) with
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}=\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}
and after simple calculations, including the fact that by the property (
6.16
):

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})

\displaystyle=b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1},\{\underline{p_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1}),\underline{\lambda_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})\})-b_{h}^{L}(\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n}),\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})

\displaystyle\leq c\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}+\epsilon\Delta t||\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{ by \eqref{eq: time differences properties bilinear b ENS}},

we can find a stability bound for (
6.46
), and subsequently for the two pressures in the
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
-norm, using the discrete
inf-sup
condition
Lemma

6.6
.
We omit further details and point to
Section

7.3
, where such analysis is carried out for proving the pressure error estimates instead.

7.
Error Analysis

In this section, we derive a-priori error bounds for the two linearized
evolving fully discrete Lagrangian surface Navier-Stokes
finite element schemes (
eNSWdh
), (
eNSWch
) with initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{0,div}
.
The error analysis follows typical arguments. We split the errors by decomposing them into an approximation error and a discrete remainder error in the appropriate finite element spaces. Initially, this is carried out with the help of the modified Ritz-Stokes projection
\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}
defined in (
5.6
) for the velocity
{\mathbf{u}}
and the standard Lagrange interpolant for the two pressures
\{p,\lambda\}
(
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
solutions of either (
eNSWd
) or (
eNSWc
)). In
Section

7.3
we consider a different decomposition, using the standard Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.7
) instead. Nontheless, we have the following:

(7.1)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}={\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{({\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n})}_{\text{Approximation error}}+\underbrace{(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n})}_{\text{discrete remainder}}:=\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}+\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},

(7.2)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{e}}_{p}^{n}=p^{n}-p_{h}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(p^{n}-\widetilde{I}_{h}p^{n})}_{\text{Approximation error}}+\underbrace{(\widetilde{I}_{h}p^{n}-p_{h})}_{\text{discrete remainder}}:=\rho_{p}^{n}+\theta_{p}^{n},

(7.3)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{e}}_{\lambda}^{n}=\lambda^{n}-\lambda_{h}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(\lambda^{n}-\widetilde{I}_{h}\lambda^{n})}_{\text{Approximation error}}+\underbrace{(\widetilde{I}_{h}\lambda^{n}-\lambda_{h})}_{\text{discrete remainder}}:=\rho_{\lambda}^{n}+\theta_{\lambda}^{n}.

The bounds of the modified Ritz-Stokes approximation error have already been proved in
Lemmas

5.3
and
5.8
. The interpolation errors for the pressures are well-known

(7.4)

\displaystyle||p^{n}-\widetilde{I}_{h}p^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq h^{k_{pr}+1}||p^{n}||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}},

\displaystyle||\lambda^{n}-\widetilde{I}_{h}\lambda^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq h^{k_{\lambda}+1}||\lambda^{n}||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}}.

Notice that for convenience, we once again drop the inverse lift extension
(\cdot)^{-\ell}
notation, since it should be clear from the context whether a quantity is defined on
\Gamma
or
\Gamma_{h}
, e.g. for functions
{\mathbf{u}},\,p,\,\lambda
.

It is clear by (
eNSWdh
) and (
eNSWch
) and the modified Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.6
) that
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
for all
n=1,...,N
. To ensure that
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
for
n=0
, the initialization of the time-stepping procedure is chosen to satisfy
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{0,div}
(see also
Section

6.2
). Although the choice of initial condition the modified Ritz-Stokes projection
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
is suitable, other approximations are possible.

Remark 7.1
.

Other choices of initial condition, see also
Section

7.3
, can be:

-

\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0})
: In this case
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}
is not weakly divergence-free so that we can only have conditional stability, and an inverse type CFL condition is necessary, see also
[
14
]
.

-

\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0},\{p^{0},\lambda^{0}\})
: The main issue is that the initial values
\{p^{0},\lambda^{0}\}
are not prescribed in the initial value problem.

In what follows, note that we abuse the notation and define
\underline{{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}(t_{n}):=(\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}(\cdot,t_{n}))^{-\ell}-\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})
, where
(\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}(\cdot,t_{n}))^{-\ell}:={\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,t_{n-1})^{-\ell}\circ\Phi^{h}_{t_{n-1}}\circ\Phi^{h}_{-t_{n}}
and
\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}:={\mathbf{u}}(t_{n-1})\circ\Phi^{\ell}_{t_{n-1}}\circ\Phi^{\ell}_{-t_{n}}
. Then, by a simple modification of the time-inequalities (
6.13
) and the properties of the map
\Phi^{h}_{t}
(
4.10
) and
\Phi^{\ell}_{t}
(
4.33
) (notice that
\partial^{\circ}_{h}\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}(\cdot,s)^{-\ell}=\Big(\frac{d}{ds}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\circ\Phi^{h}(\cdot,t_{n-1})\Big)\circ\Phi^{h}(\cdot,s)=0
for
s\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
) , it is not difficult to see that

(7.5)

\displaystyle||(\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})^{-\ell}(\cdot,t_{n})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq c||({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1})^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},\qquad||(\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})^{-\ell}(\cdot,t_{n})||_{a_{h}}\leq c||({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1})^{-\ell}||_{a_{h}},

\displaystyle||\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}(\cdot,t_{n})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{n-1})},\qquad\qquad\qquad||\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}(\cdot,t_{n})||_{a}\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{a},

therefore it also holds that

(7.6)

||\underline{{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\leq||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},\qquad||\underline{{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}(\cdot,t_{n})||_{a_{h}}\leq||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}.

From now on, we also omit the space argument in the
time-lift
(
6.4
).

We will make use of the following regularity assumption.

Assumptions 1

(Regularity I)
.

We assume that the solution
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
has the following regularity

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}||\partial^{\circ}{{\mathbf{u}}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}\,dt+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\{||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle+||p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}+||\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}\big\}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}||\partial^{\circ}\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}+||\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}\,dt\leq C.

7.1.
A-priori Velocity Error Bounds

Recall that by
c^{(\cdot)}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
we denote either the convective term
c_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
or
c^{cov}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
, and thus consider either the numerical scheme (
eNSWdh
) or (
eNSWch
), respectively.
Using the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.6
)
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n,div}
for
n=0,1,...,N
,the discrete pressure error decouples from the velocity error. This is crucial to find error bounds for
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}
independently of
\theta_{p}^{n},\,\theta_{\lambda}^{n}
. The following error equations hold.

Lemma 7.1
.

For
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\,\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}
as in (
7.1
), (
7.2
), (
7.3
) the following error equations holds true for
n\geq 1
:

(EQ)

\displaystyle\boxed{\begin{cases}&{\dfrac{1}{\Delta t}\Big({\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(\cdot,t^{n-1}))\Big)}+a_{S,h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ ~~=\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{5}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})}_{\textsc{Cons. Errors}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{I}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})}_{\textsc{Inter. Errors}}+\!\!\!\!\!\underbrace{\mathcal{C}^{(\cdot)}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})}_{\textsc{Inert. Errors}},\\
&b_{h}^{L}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\})=0,\end{cases}}

for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{n}
and
\{q_{h}^{n},\xi_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}^{n}\times\Lambda_{h}^{n}
,
with
consistency errors
:

•

\textsc{Err}_{1}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}):=\dfrac{1}{\Delta t}\Big({\mathbf{m}}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(t_{n-1}))\Big)-{\mathbf{m}}(\partial^{\circ}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})^{\ell})-{\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})

•

\textsc{Err}_{2}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}):=b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{p^{n},\lambda^{n}\})-b^{L}((\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})^{\ell},\{p^{n},\lambda^{n}\})

•

\textsc{Err}_{3}^{C,(\cdot)}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}):=c_{h}^{(\cdot)}(\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}(t_{n});{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-c^{(\cdot)}({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})^{\ell})

•

\textsc{Err}_{4}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}):={\mathbf{m}}({\bm{f}}^{n},(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})^{\ell})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})

•

\textsc{Err}_{5}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}):=a_{S,h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-a_{S}({\mathbf{u}}^{n},(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})^{\ell})

and
interpolations errors
:

•

\textsc{Err}_{1}^{I}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}):=-\dfrac{1}{\Delta t}\Big({\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(t_{n-1}))\Big)

•

\textsc{Err}_{2}^{I}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}):=-b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{\rho_{p}^{n},\rho_{\lambda}^{n}\})

•

•

\mathcal{C}^{(\cdot)}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}):=c_{h}^{(\cdot)}(\underline{{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}(t_{n});{\mathbf{u}}^{n};\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-c_{h}^{(\cdot)}(\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(t_{n});{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n};\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})

Proof.

Considering the decompositions (
7.1
), (
7.2
), (
7.3
) and the modified Ritz-Stokes map (
5.6
), and using the finite element approximations (
eNSWdh
) and (
eNSWch
), we derive the following

\displaystyle(\mathrm{EQ}):=-\dfrac{1}{\Delta t}\Big({\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(t_{n-1}))\Big)+a_{S,h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\})=-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{g}}_{h}({\mathbf{V}}_{\Gamma_{h}}^{N};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})+\dfrac{1}{\Delta t}\Big({\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-{\mathbf{m}}_{h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1},\underline{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}}(t_{n-1}))\Big)+a_{S,h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})

\displaystyle+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}p^{n},\widetilde{I}_{h}\lambda^{n}\})+c_{h}^{(\cdot)}(\underline{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}(t_{n});\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n};\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}).

Now recalling continuous weak formulations (
eNSWd
) and (
eNSWc
) and adding and subtracting the term
c_{h}^{(\cdot)}(\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}(t_{n});{\mathbf{u}}^{n};\mathbf{v}_{h})
